Set for release in the U.S. and South Korea on Dec. 10 with pre-orders beginning today, the collection combines Samsung’s mobile engineering expertise with 30 distinctive designs from […] <p>Samsung Electronics today announced the upcoming launch of the full lineup of intelligent eyewear, delivering a smarter way to see the world through intuitive, <strong>hands-free AI assistance</strong>. Set for release in the U.S. and South Korea on Dec. 10 with pre-orders beginning today, the collection combines Samsung’s mobile engineering expertise with 30 distinctive designs<sup data-fn="29ad368b-3072-46d5-96f0-9f25fc104f27" class="fn"><a href="#29ad368b-3072-46d5-96f0-9f25fc104f27" id="29ad368b-3072-46d5-96f0-9f25fc104f27-link">1</a></sup> from <strong>Gentle Monster</strong> and <strong>Warby Parker</strong> for comfortable, stylish everyday wear. Developed in collaboration with Google and with <strong>Gemini </strong>built in, the intelligent eyewear helps users understand the world around them, communicate across languages and stay connected while remaining present in the moment. Responsibly designed with everyone’s comfort in mind, the intelligent eyewear has controls that put the user in charge and has privacy protections for both the wearer and people around them.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img width="1000" height="491" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/12152225/Samsung-Mobile-Intelligent-Eyewear-Launch-Gentle-Monster-Warby-Parker_main1.jpg" alt="Samsung Electronics today announced the upcoming launch of the full lineup of intelligent eyewear, delivering a smarter way to see the world through intuitive, hands-free AI assistance." class="wp-image-178323" /></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Designed for Style and Everyday Comfort</strong></h2>



<p>Gentle Monster and Warby Parker bring their design identities to a combined lineup of 30 designs, offering customers a wide range of frame shapes, colors, sizes, and lens treatments to match their personal style. Gentle Monster brings bold, disruptive aesthetics, while Warby Parker offers timeless designs with a refined appeal.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img width="1000" height="373" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/12152250/Samsung-Mobile-Intelligent-Eyewear-Launch-Gentle-Monster-Warby-Parker_main2.jpg" alt="The six frame designs of Gentle Monster Intelligent Eyewear with various colorways" class="wp-image-178324" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The six frame designs of Gentle Monster Intelligent Eyewear with various colorways</figcaption></figure>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img width="1000" height="376" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/12152327/Samsung-Mobile-Intelligent-Eyewear-Launch-Gentle-Monster-Warby-Parker_main3.jpg" alt="The three frame designs of Warby Parker Intelligent Eyewear in optical and sun versions" class="wp-image-178325" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The three frame designs of Warby Parker Intelligent Eyewear in optical and sun versions</figcaption></figure>



<p>Samsung’s engineering expertise brings together the advanced hardware required for intelligent eyewear in <strong>slim, lightweight frames</strong> without sacrificing durability or comfort. Weighing around 41 grams,<sup data-fn="5c8538bc-9402-4255-91d1-252d56043424" class="fn"><a href="#5c8538bc-9402-4255-91d1-252d56043424" id="5c8538bc-9402-4255-91d1-252d56043424-link">2</a></sup> the eyewear was carefully designed to balance weight, structural strength, and comfort, making it suitable for extended use throughout the day.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>More Intuitive Ways To Interact With the World</strong></h2>



<p>With Gemini built in, the intelligent eyewear provides intuitive, hands-free experiences. It is designed to help users stay connected and present while offering a range of capabilities:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Explore and understand surroundings:</strong> Users can ask questions about what they see<sup data-fn="61778b9e-adb3-4da9-a80d-cfbefe4a29ba" class="fn"><a href="#61778b9e-adb3-4da9-a80d-cfbefe4a29ba" id="61778b9e-adb3-4da9-a80d-cfbefe4a29ba-link">3</a></sup> — such as identifying an unfamiliar landmark or learning about an object — continuing the conversation naturally with follow-up questions.</li>



<li><strong>Enhance daily productivity: </strong>The intelligent eyewear helps users turn information into action. For example, a user can capture a whiteboard during a meeting, ask Gemini to summarize the key takeaways and save them directly into Samsung Notes.</li>



<li><strong>Translate and navigate on the go:</strong><sup data-fn="2a9766b1-91fd-42b2-83e5-fe069a3c351a" class="fn"><a href="#2a9766b1-91fd-42b2-83e5-fe069a3c351a" id="2a9766b1-91fd-42b2-83e5-fe069a3c351a-link">4</a></sup> Users can translate a restaurant menu or find their way around unfamiliar places with turn-by-turn directions from Google Maps.</li>



<li><strong>Stay connected, hands-free:</strong> The intelligent eyewear allows users to effortlessly stay connected. It can automatically prioritize, group, and summarize notifications based on context, read important updates aloud and enable natural voice responses. Whether making calls or responding to messages while their hands are occupied, users can stay connected on the go.</li>
</ul>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Where Innovation Meets Craftsmanship</strong></h2>



<p>Behind these intelligent experiences is purpose-built hardware to support natural, hands-free interactions. A <strong>12MP HDR camera</strong> provides visual context for Gemini while supporting high-resolution photos and clear video recording. Open-ear speakers and a four-microphone array enable clear voice interactions, calls and audio playback while maintaining awareness of the user’s surroundings.</p>



<p>Powered by the <strong>Snapdragon® AR1 Gen 1 Platform,</strong><sup data-fn="5b5eac1f-b92f-4b5c-8f29-09ac5ab75b37" class="fn"><a href="#5b5eac1f-b92f-4b5c-8f29-09ac5ab75b37" id="5b5eac1f-b92f-4b5c-8f29-09ac5ab75b37-link">5</a></sup> the intelligent eyewear delivers smooth performance with up to <strong>nine hours of battery</strong> life on a single charge, and up to 72 hours with a fully charged charging case.<sup data-fn="eeff538e-5fd9-4b94-936a-1118595410d1" class="fn"><a href="#eeff538e-5fd9-4b94-936a-1118595410d1" id="eeff538e-5fd9-4b94-936a-1118595410d1-link">6</a></sup> Designed to work in tandem with Galaxy phones, the intelligent eyewear utilizes a split-computing system to offload a portion of the processing load to the connected device. This approach, combined with Samsung’s careful optimization of component placement, battery design, and thermal management, helps balance performance, durability and everyday comfort in a lightweight form factor.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Responsibly Designed for Everyone’s Comfort</strong></h2>



<p>The intelligent eyewear was responsibly designed with the comfort and privacy of everyone in mind, recognizing the need to consider both the people wearing it and those around them. This commitment has shaped key decisions from the outset, with transparency and user control built into the experience.</p>



<p>Responsible design is built into the practical experience:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Clear recording indicators:</strong> When capturing images or recording video for your gallery, an LED indicates that the camera is active, while the wearer simultaneously receives a visual notification via an inner light and a sound. Built-in safeguards are designed to disable camera capture if the external LED is covered or not functioning properly.</li>



<li><strong>Considerate audio design:</strong> Open-ear speakers are designed to minimize sound leakage while helping wearers remain aware of their surroundings. In conversations with Gemini Live, surrounding sounds, including other people’s voices, are filtered to help protect bystander privacy.</li>



<li><strong>User control and data privacy:</strong> Users retain control over both app-specific permissions and their Gemini activity. Visuals collected by Gemini are not used for AI model training or ad targeting. When users ask Gemini about what they’re looking at, the intelligent eyewear is designed to automatically redact bystanders’ faces before responding.</li>



<li><strong>Responsible use:</strong> The intelligent eyewear is designed for users aged 18 and over, with age verification required for device activation and app downloads.</li>
</ul>



<p>For more detailed information on the privacy policies for the intelligent eyewear, please visit <a href="https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/technology/safety-security/intelligent-eyewear-privacy-protections" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Google’s privacy blog post</a>.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Availability</strong></h2>



<p>Gentle Monster Intelligent Eyewear and Warby Parker Intelligent Eyewear will be available in select markets<sup data-fn="6c65ebb5-c850-4598-873b-a13a362ec9c8" class="fn"><a href="#6c65ebb5-c850-4598-873b-a13a362ec9c8" id="6c65ebb5-c850-4598-873b-a13a362ec9c8-link">7</a></sup> beginning Dec. 10, 2026. Both collections will launch in the U.S., while Gentle Monster Intelligent Eyewear will also be available in Korea.</p>



<p>Pre-orders<sup data-fn="4c874c10-dd74-4285-bd82-c815600aaddd" class="fn"><a href="#4c874c10-dd74-4285-bd82-c815600aaddd" id="4c874c10-dd74-4285-bd82-c815600aaddd-link">8</a></sup> for both collections begin today, ahead of general availability. Customers will be able to experience and purchase the intelligent eyewear across Warby Parker’s retail and online channels, with purchases also available through Gentle Monster’s official online store. Hands-on demonstrations will also be available at select Samsung retail locations from Dec. 10.</p>



<p>In the U.S., Warby Parker Intelligent Eyewear will start at $395, while Gentle Monster Intelligent Eyewear will range from $449 to $489, depending on the model.</p>



<p>For more information on the intelligent eyewear, please visit <a href="https://www.samsung.com/us/intelligent-eyewear/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Samsung.com</a>.</p>



<p></p>

AI capabilities now go beyond picture and sound optimization to support […] <p>Choosing the right TV is a long-term decision that involves several factors — including screen size, picture quality, brightness, viewing environment, and preferred content. </p>



<p>Advances in TV technology have expanded the options available, from OLED, QLED, and Neo QLED to the latest Micro RGB. AI capabilities now go beyond picture and sound optimization to support content discovery, device connectivity, and personalized viewing experiences. As a result, consumers are asking more specific questions about screen size, AI features, and their viewing needs rather than simply “Which TV is the best?” </p>



<p>Samsung Newsroom outlines key criteria and answers common questions about buying a TV.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>How To Choose the Right TV Size</strong> </h2>



<p>When choosing a TV size, viewing distance and available space are key considerations. For comfortable, immersive viewing, the recommended distance is approximately 1.2 times the screen size in centimeters, based on a 40-degree field of view.<sup data-fn="5f56ebb0-9fa7-4dce-a629-07116f95b05f" class="fn"><a href="#5f56ebb0-9fa7-4dce-a629-07116f95b05f" id="5f56ebb0-9fa7-4dce-a629-07116f95b05f-link">1</a></sup></p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="564" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/11133721/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Finding-the-perfect-TV-A-Guide-Samsung-TVs_Main1.jpg" alt="Recommended viewing distance by TV screen size" class="wp-image-178297" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Recommended viewing distance by TV screen size</figcaption></figure>



<p>For typical living room seating arrangements, recommended screen sizes vary by viewing distance: </p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li>1.5 to 2.0 meters: 43- to 55-inch TVs </li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li>2.0 to 2.5 meters: 55- to 65-inch TVs </li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li>2.3 to 3.0 meters: 65- to 75-inch TVs </li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li>3.0 meters or more: 85-inch or larger TVs </li>
</ul>



<p></p>



<p>These are general guidelines, as comfortable viewing distances may vary depending on viewing habits — such as watching content with subtitles or as a family. </p>



<p>Larger screens enhance immersion but may cause eye strain if too large for the space. Samsung offers TVs ranging from 32 to 114 inches to accommodate different spaces and viewing preferences. </p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>How Lighting Affects TV Selection</strong> </h2>



<p>Viewing times and lighting conditions vary from home to home, making the viewing environment an important consideration when choosing a TV. In bright spaces, screen brightness and reflection reduction are key, while contrast ratio matters more in darker settings. </p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="614" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/11134122/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Finding-the-perfect-TV-A-Guide-Samsung-TVs_Main2.jpg" alt="Samsung Neo QLED 4K delivers bright, vivid picture quality in well-lit spaces, while Samsung OLED TV offers deep blacks and exceptional contrast in dark environments." class="wp-image-178298" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Samsung Neo QLED 4K delivers bright, vivid picture quality in well-lit spaces, while Samsung OLED TV offers deep blacks and exceptional contrast in dark environments.</figcaption></figure>



<p>For sunlit living rooms, Neo QLED TVs provide high brightness for clear viewing. OLED TVs, meanwhile, deliver exceptional contrast — making them well suited to darker spaces. </p>



<p>However, advances in display technology are making lighting conditions less of a constraint. OLED TVs with Glare Free technology minimize reflections, maintaining a clear picture even in brightly lit rooms.<sup data-fn="6dc04f4d-e574-4a6e-9fc0-9ad32b5865f7" class="fn"><a href="#6dc04f4d-e574-4a6e-9fc0-9ad32b5865f7" id="6dc04f4d-e574-4a6e-9fc0-9ad32b5865f7-link">2</a></sup>  </p>



<p>AI Gamma technology analyzes scene brightness in real time and automatically adjusts gamma levels based on ambient lighting. It brightens dark scenes to preserve detail in well-lit rooms and reduces glare while enhancing contrast in darker settings. These adjustments help minimize eye strain without compromising picture clarity. </p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>How Samsung TV Display Technologies Compare</strong> </h2>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="574" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/11134332/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Finding-the-perfect-TV-A-Guide-Samsung-TVs_Main3.jpg" alt="Each display technology offers a distinct viewing experience, with unique strengths in brightness, contrast, and color expression." class="wp-image-178299" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Each display technology offers a distinct viewing experience, with unique strengths in brightness, contrast, and color expression.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Display technologies produce light and color differently, each offering advantages depending on viewing conditions and intended use: </p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>OLED:</strong> Relies on self-emissive pixels rather than a backlight to deliver near-perfect blacks and fast response times. </li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Mini LED:</strong> Employs smaller LEDs than conventional backlights to produce strong contrast, particularly for HDR content. </li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Neo QLED:</strong> Combines Quantum Matrix Technology for precise Quantum Mini LED backlight control with Quantum Dot technology that accurately reproduces 2,140 Pantone colors and 110 skin tones. </li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Micro RGB:</strong> Features individually controlled, microscopic red, green, and blue (RGB) backlights for a wide color gamut and precise color reproduction. </li>
</ul>



<p>OLED TVs excel at reproducing deep blacks, making them ideal for movies and TV shows in dark rooms. In brighter spaces, TVs with advanced backlight control have an advantage. Mini LED TVs provide strong contrast at an affordable price, while premium Neo QLED TVs combine rich colors from Samsung’s proprietary quantum dot film with powerful AI upscaling. For vivid, accurate colors and lifelike picture quality faithful to the original content, Micro RGB TVs are another option. </p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>How Content Shapes TV Choices</strong> </h2>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="574" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/11135010/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Finding-the-perfect-TV-A-Guide-Samsung-TVs_Main4.jpg" alt="Samsung TVs deliver an optimized viewing experience for different types of content, including movies, sports, and games." class="wp-image-178301" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Samsung TVs deliver an optimized viewing experience for different types of content, including movies, sports, and games.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Different types of content call for different TV features: </p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Movies:</strong> High Dynamic Range (HDR) brings out subtle details and colors in both bright and dark scenes, creating greater depth and realism. </li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Live Sports:</strong> AI-powered features enhance the viewing experience through real-time picture and sound adjustments. AI Soccer Mode Pro, AI Motion Enhancer, and AI Sound Controller Pro work together to deliver clearer action and more immersive stadium sound. </li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Video Games:</strong> High refresh rates ensure smooth motion, while low input lag enables faster responses to player commands. AI Gaming Optimizer automatically adjusts picture and sound settings based on the game genre. </li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Art:</strong> Samsung’s Art TV lineup, including The Frame and The Frame Pro, displays renowned artworks when not in use — transforming living spaces into galleries. Customizable bezels, the One Connect Box, and Wireless One Connect create a picture frame-like appearance, while Samsung Art Store provides access to more than 5,000 artworks through a subscription. </li>
</ul>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>How AI Makes TVs Smarter</strong> </h2>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="606" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/11135535/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Finding-the-perfect-TV-A-Guide-Samsung-TVs_Main5.jpg" alt="Samsung AI TVs allow users to adjust dialogue and background audio in content to suit their preferences." class="wp-image-178304" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Samsung AI TVs allow users to adjust dialogue and background audio in content to suit their preferences.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Unlike conventional TVs, Samsung AI TVs automatically optimize picture and sound while supporting content discovery and information searches. </p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">

<p>When choosing a TV, consumers should look for AI features that match their viewing habits — from automatic picture and sound optimization for sports, movies, and games to on-screen information searches. </p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>TV Buying Guide at a Glance</strong></h2>



<p><strong>Q. What TV size is ideal for a living room?</strong> </p>



<p>For viewing distances of 2.3 meters or more, a 75-inch or larger TV is recommended. With 4K resolution now widespread, larger screens maintain sharp picture quality even at closer distances. However, sufficient installation space and viewing distance remain important.</p>



<p><strong>Q. What TV size is ideal for a bedroom?</strong> </p>



<p>Bedrooms typically have shorter viewing distances and limited space, making 42- to 50-inch TVs generally suitable. The ideal size depends on the distance from the bed to the screen, as well as the TV’s overall dimensions including the stand and bezel.</p>



<p><strong>Q. Should consumers choose a 65- or 75-inch TV?</strong> </p>



<p>The recommended viewing distance is approximately 2.0 meters for a 65-inch TV and 2.3 meters for a 75-inch model. If possible, compare both sizes from the intended viewing distances before deciding. </p>



<p><strong>Q. Can lower-resolution content still look sharp on a 4K TV?</strong> </p>



<p>Yes. AI Upscaling enhances lower-resolution content to match 4K displays for sharper viewing. Results may vary by model and source material. </p>



<p><strong>Q. Do OLED TVs reduce glare?</strong> </p>



<p>OLED TVs combine exceptional contrast with Glare Free technology to minimize reflections. The 2025 Samsung S95F OLED TV features a low-reflection layer that diffuses external light while preserving contrast and color, even in bright rooms. </p>



<p><strong>Q. Should consumers choose QLED or Neo QLED TVs?</strong> </p>



<p>Neo QLED TVs provide high brightness and precise contrast, while QLED TVs deliver vivid colors at a more affordable price. Both use Quantum Dot technology, but Neo QLED TVs feature smaller Mini LED backlights for more precise backlight control. </p>



<p><strong>Q. What is the difference between HDR and non-HDR TVs?</strong> </p>



<p>HDR TVs reproduce a wider range of brightness and preserve details in highlights and shadows, creating greater depth and realism. </p>



<p><strong>Q. Can I use a Samsung Smart TV without a subscription?</strong> </p>



<p>Yes. Samsung Smart TVs offer features and free content without a paid subscription. Models supporting Samsung TV Plus provide free channels featuring news, entertainment, dramas, and movies.<sup data-fn="75442f7a-441c-41ef-83c0-1b46069fc8c6" class="fn"><a href="#75442f7a-441c-41ef-83c0-1b46069fc8c6" id="75442f7a-441c-41ef-83c0-1b46069fc8c6-link">3</a></sup> </p>



<p>Samsung Art Store allows users to select artworks and the monthly curated Art Store Streams collection for free. A subscription unlocks access to the full collection of more than 5,000 artworks.<sup data-fn="8b355981-ad44-4f7d-bc1d-2d11cd62a0a1" class="fn"><a href="#8b355981-ad44-4f7d-bc1d-2d11cd62a0a1" id="8b355981-ad44-4f7d-bc1d-2d11cd62a0a1-link">4</a></sup> </p>



<p><strong>Q. Which is better for gaming, OLED or Neo QLED TVs?</strong> </p>



<p>OLED TVs deliver fast response times and near-perfect blacks, while Neo QLED TVs provide high brightness and strong HDR performance. Both offer high refresh rates, low input lag, and Game Modes, making room lighting and preferred game genres important considerations. </p>



<p><strong>Q. How can theater-quality sound be achieved with a Samsung TV?</strong> </p>



<p>Q-Symphony enables compatible Samsung TVs and sound devices to play audio together, delivering optimized surround sound based on room layout and device placement. </p>

The innovators are sharing solutions they have developed to address social challenges in their communities. Launched in 2010, Solve for […] <p>Samsung Electronics and the International Olympic Committee (IOC) are spotlighting young innovators from Samsung Solve for Tomorrow (SFT) programme at the Youth Olympic Games Dakar 2026, the first Olympic Games to be held in Africa. The innovators are sharing solutions they have developed to address social challenges in their communities.</p>



<p>Launched in 2010, Solve for Tomorrow is Samsung’s global corporate social responsibility (CSR) program for young people, helping them use science, technology, engineering, and mathematics (STEM) to address challenges in their communities. Through education and mentoring, students learn to identify problems, develop ideas, and build and refine prototypes. The global ambassador initiative gives them opportunities to present their work internationally.</p>



<p>The activities in Dakar build on Samsung and the IOC’s collaboration on SFT’s Sport & Tech theme and their joint delivery of the Samsung Solve for Tomorrow Impact Programme. On an Olympic stage where young athletes challenge themselves and grow, the two organizations are giving young innovators an opportunity to present their ideas, learn from others and take the next steps in their development.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/11132335/Samsung-CSR-Samsung-Solve-for-Tomorrow-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Young-Innovators_main1.jpg" alt="Samsung Solve for Tomorrow students pose in front of the booth." class="wp-image-178286" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Samsung Solve for Tomorrow students pose in front of the booth.</figcaption></figure>



<p>“The commitment we made together with the IOC at Milano-Cortina 2026 Olympic Winter Games was a promise to give young people opportunities to discover what they can achieve through technology and sport,” said Eddie Cho, Head of Corporate Citizenship at Samsung Electronics. “In Dakar, that promise starts to take shape. The ambassadors began by tackling problems in their schools and neighborhoods; now they are sharing their solutions with the world. We will continue this collaboration with the IOC to help young people build on what they learn, gain confidence and make a greater difference in their communities.”</p>



<p>“The Youth Olympic Games are about much more than competition. They are an opportunity for young people to challenge themselves, learn from one another and experience the Olympic values of friendship, respect and excellence,” said James Macleod, Olympism365 Director, IOC. “Together with Samsung, we are committed to supporting young people take that spirit beyond the field of play and into their everyday lives and communities. Bringing Solve for Tomorrow ambassadors together with the youth in Dakar is a wonderful opportunity for them to share their ideas, learn from each other and see what is possible when creativity, technology and sport come together. We hope they leave Dakar inspired by one another, with new connections and even greater confidence in their ability to make a positive difference.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/11132401/Samsung-CSR-Samsung-Solve-for-Tomorrow-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Young-Innovators_main2.jpg" alt="James Macleod, IOC Olympism365 Director, speaks at the opening event." class="wp-image-178287" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ James Macleod, IOC Olympism365 Director, speaks at the opening event.</figcaption></figure>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Three Solve for Tomorrow Teams Experience Dakar 2026</strong></h2>



<p>“We wanted to do something about the problems we saw around us,” said Ela Aksoy of Oilsorb, Türkiye. “Through Samsung Solve for Tomorrow, we learned technical skills and worked with mentors to keep improving our idea until it became a working solution. We are proud to demonstrate what we have built in Dakar.”</p>



<p>“Taking part in Solve for Tomorrow in Dakar has been such a fun experience,” said Kukhanya Mbebe of Brick Beast, South Africa. “I really enjoyed meeting students from different countries, hearing about their projects and sharing our own experiences. My favourite part was getting to know the other students and exchanging ideas. We came from different places, but it was easy to connect through our projects.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/11132434/Samsung-CSR-Samsung-Solve-for-Tomorrow-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Young-Innovators_main3.jpg" alt="The Brick Beast, the 2026 Solve for Tomorrow winning team from South Africa, presents its solution." class="wp-image-178289" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The Brick Beast, the 2026 Solve for Tomorrow winning team from South Africa, presents its solution.</figcaption></figure>



<p>“We want to use the feedback we receive here to develop technology that can help more people,” said Ela Aksoy. “I’m taking home new ideas and great memories from Dakar,” said Kukhanya Mbebe.</p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Solve for Tomorrow Exhibition at Dakar 2026</strong></h2>



<p>At the SFT exhibition, organized in cooperation with the IOC at the Corniche West Live Site, visitors could explore the SFT ambassadors’ solutions and hear about the challenges they faced and how they refined their ideas. By discussing their work and exchanging feedback with people from different backgrounds, these young people can consider how their technology might serve a wider range of needs. The exhibition is part of a wider conversation and shared learning experience at Dakar 2026. The exhibition is scheduled to run through November 13, 2026.</p>



<p>During the exhibition opening, through the panel discussion “Youth at the Heart of Global Transformation”, Samsung shared with the Senegalese students the SFT programme’s objectives to encourage young people to solve problems through technology and sport, highlighting how young people can use sport and the Olympic spirit in taking on challenges and working together in everyday life.</p>



<p>As part of the programme the ambassadors also met with IOC Young Leaders from Kenya and Lebanon for a knowledge exchange workshop with focus on social challenges of young people in Africa. SFT students who have used technology to tackle local problems will exchange experiences with IOC Young Leaders who have used sport to bring about change in their communities.</p>



<p>After the workshop, the students have also visited the “Olympic Social Impact” booth, presented by IOC Young Leaders, in the Youth Olympic Village to learn about athlete experience engagement. </p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>From Paris to LA, Supporting the Next Steps for Young Innovators</strong></h2>



<p>Samsung and the IOC first introduced SFT ambassadors at Paris 2024 and affirmed their shared commitment to developing future talent at Milano Cortina 2026 and beyond. In August 2026, they jointly launched the Solve for Tomorrow Impact Programme to support young innovators in developing their ideas and putting them to use in their communities and this programme will run until end of January 2028.</p>



<p>As the Samsung’s Olympic partnership continues toward the LA28 Games, Samsung and the IOC plan to sustain their collaboration on SFT’s Sport & Tech theme and the SFT Impact Program.</p> Samsung Gallery Makes It Easier To Access Photos and Videos Across Galaxy Devices https://news.samsung.com/global/samsung-gallery-makes-it-easier-to-access-photos-and-videos-across-galaxy-devices Tue, 10 Nov 2026 21:00:00 +0000 Samsung Newsroom Mobile Google storage Photo sync Samsung Gallery https://bit.ly/3WsIZ7D As people capture, store, and revisit memories across multiple devices like mobile phones and tablets, keeping those moments safe and easily accessible has become increasingly important. To help meet this growing need, Samsung Electronics offers a Gallery sync service in collaboration with Google. Samsung Gallery sync with Google storage gives you access to photos and […] <p>As people capture, store, and revisit memories across multiple devices like mobile phones and tablets, keeping those moments safe and easily accessible has become increasingly important. To help meet this growing need, Samsung Electronics offers a Gallery sync service in collaboration with Google.<sup data-fn="1c482d23-4925-4698-ae4c-eb874ebf87c8" class="fn"><a href="#1c482d23-4925-4698-ae4c-eb874ebf87c8" id="1c482d23-4925-4698-ae4c-eb874ebf87c8-link">1</a></sup></p>



<p>Samsung Gallery sync with Google storage gives you access to photos and videos across supported Galaxy devices. Getting started is simple. If your Samsung and Google accounts are set up on the device, you can enable sync in Gallery without creating a separate cloud account.</p>



<p>You can also move easily between devices while keeping your photos and videos within reach. For example, take photos on your Galaxy phone and view them in Gallery on your tablet. Sign in with the same Samsung and Google accounts and enable sync on each supported device.<sup data-fn="6b0144f9-5b85-4f43-9f42-b16fcae01497" class="fn"><a href="#6b0144f9-5b85-4f43-9f42-b16fcae01497" id="6b0144f9-5b85-4f43-9f42-b16fcae01497-link">2</a></sup></p>



<p>When setting up a new Galaxy device, sign in with the same Samsung and Google accounts used for Gallery sync on your previous device. Turn on sync to see previews of your photos and videos.<sup data-fn="a57fe468-d4f4-4fbe-a1ec-eaf7369a9d6e" class="fn"><a href="#a57fe468-d4f4-4fbe-a1ec-eaf7369a9d6e" id="a57fe468-d4f4-4fbe-a1ec-eaf7369a9d6e-link">3</a></sup> If you need an original file, you can select the file you want and download it to your device easily.</p>



<p>To help you make room for more memories, Samsung introduces a new feature that lets you free up space on your device.<sup data-fn="c0fb60d0-5284-46cb-ba63-5e8ab3b42651" class="fn"><a href="#c0fb60d0-5284-46cb-ba63-5e8ab3b42651" id="c0fb60d0-5284-46cb-ba63-5e8ab3b42651-link">4</a></sup> Once photos and videos are synced to Google storage, you can use the “Free up space” feature to remove the original files from the device and free up device storage, while keeping thumbnails visible in Gallery.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/09131634/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Gallery-Google-storage-Gallery-sync_main1_F.jpg" alt=""Free up space" feature" class="wp-image-178259" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ “Free up space” feature</figcaption></figure>



<p>Gallery sync uses your Google account’s available storage, which is shared with Gmail, Google Drive, and Google Photos. Google accounts include up to 15 GB at no cost.<sup data-fn="ec5e88b6-2847-40f5-a788-9d61aeba9a01" class="fn"><a href="#ec5e88b6-2847-40f5-a788-9d61aeba9a01" id="ec5e88b6-2847-40f5-a788-9d61aeba9a01-link">5</a></sup></p>



<p>Support has started with select Galaxy devices running One UI 9 and will expand in phases. To set up sync, tap the Cloud icon in Gallery or tap “Get started” on the tip card or go to Gallery Settings, turn on “Sync photos and videos,” and follow the on-screen instructions.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/09131849/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Gallery-Google-storage-Gallery-sync_main2.jpg" alt="How to set up Samsung Gallery sync with Google storage" class="wp-image-178262" /></figure>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/09132253/Samsung-Mobile-Samsung-Gallery-Google-storage-Gallery-sync_main3.jpg" alt="How to set up Samsung Gallery sync with Google storage" class="wp-image-178264" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ How to set up Samsung Gallery sync with Google storage</figcaption></figure>

As a Worldwide Olympic and Paralympic Partner, Samsung Electronics is supporting the Games with Galaxy technology — from […] <p>The Youth Olympic Games Dakar 2026 are under way in Senegal from October 31 to November 13, bringing together some 2,700 young athletes across 25 sports for the first Olympic event ever held on the African continent.</p>



<p>As a Worldwide Olympic and Paralympic Partner, Samsung Electronics is supporting the Games with Galaxy technology — from Victory Selfie and broadcast integrations to the Samsung Open Station, an experience space inside the Youth Olympic Village. Having supported the Youth Olympic Games since the inaugural edition in Singapore in 2010, Samsung is going beyond technology at Dakar 2026 to support young athletes’ personal growth as well.</p>



<p>For the athletes competing at Dakar 2026, reaching the Olympic stage is the result of years of dedication. But some of the most lasting lessons come from what happens along the way — overcoming setbacks, building resilience, discovering what matters beyond results.</p>



<p>That is why, on November 3, Samsung hosted “Beyond the Medal: Your Journey Shapes What Comes Next,” a mentoring session at the Samsung Open Station inside the Youth Olympic Village. By bringing in athletes who have walked the Olympic path before them, the session aimed to inspire pride and motivation in the next generation — and to share the spirit of openness at the heart of Samsung’s Olympic partnership.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/04144046/Samsung-Mobile-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Mentoring-session_main1.jpg" alt="The mentoring session held at the Samsung Open Station (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)" class="wp-image-178226" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The mentoring session held at the Samsung Open Station (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)</figcaption></figure>



<p>Two IOC Athlete Role Models<sup data-fn="4e413af0-326b-42dc-b4d8-97c4dc6e1e1b" class="fn"><a href="#4e413af0-326b-42dc-b4d8-97c4dc6e1e1b" id="4e413af0-326b-42dc-b4d8-97c4dc6e1e1b-link">1</a></sup> — Senegalese canoe slalom Olympian Jean-Pierre Bourhis (31) and Ugandan swimmer Jamila Lunkuse (29) — joined New Zealand tennis bronze medalist Marcus Daniell (36) for a conversation with young athletes.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="542" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/04144405/Samsung-Mobile-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Mentoring-session_main2.jpg" alt="(From left) Jean-Pierre Bourhis (Senegal, Slalom canoe), Jamila Lunkuse (Uganda, Swimming), and Marcus Daniell (New Zealand, Tennis) (Copyright: Getty Images)" class="wp-image-178227" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ (From left) Jean-Pierre Bourhis (Senegal, Slalom canoe), Jamila Lunkuse (Uganda, Swimming), and Marcus Daniell (New Zealand, Tennis) (Copyright: Getty Images)</figcaption></figure>



<p>Moderated by <a href="https://news.samsung.com/global/video-voices-of-galaxy-meet-lefa-makgato-championing-africas-next-generation-of-innovators" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Lefa Makgato</a>, CSR Manager at Samsung Electronics Africa, the session brought the three mentors together with 28 young athletes from 12 countries to share the journeys that shaped them — and offer a guide for exploring where life beyond sport might lead.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Open the Way: Finding Your Own Path</strong></h2>



<p>Makgato opened by asking each mentor where their Olympic journey began.</p>



<p>For Bourhis, who represented Senegal at Rio 2016 and Tokyo 2020 and is featured in Samsung’s “<a href="https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zFuVGbyOgSs" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Voices of Galaxy: Paddling for the Future</a>,” the dream took shape in 2008, when Togo’s Benjamin Boukpeti won a bronze medal in kayaking at Beijing 2008.</p>



<p>“As a kid, I realized an African paddler could reach the podium,” he said. “Rio 2016 showed me the way could be opened. Now my job is to keep it open for others.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/04144427/Samsung-Mobile-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Mentoring-session_main3.jpg" alt="" class="wp-image-178228" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Jean-Pierre Bourhis (Senegal), IOC Athlete Role Model at Samsung Mentoring Session (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)</figcaption></figure>



<p>Lunkuse, who made her Olympic Games debut at 15, recalled that swimming began with friendship rather than ambition. “I wasn’t thinking about medals or the Olympics,” she said. “I just enjoyed being part of something with my friends, and that’s what built my love for the sport.”</p>



<p>Daniell, who ended New Zealand’s 109-year wait for an Olympic tennis medal at Tokyo 2020, pointed to choosing tennis over football at 14. “Because I’d given up a sport I loved, I knew I had to fully commit,” he said.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Break Your Boundaries: Learning Through Challenges</strong></h2>



<p>The conversation then turned to the biggest challenges each mentor faced.</p>



<p>With almost no whitewater facilities in Senegal, Bourhis had to build his training base in France — while holding down a full-time job as an engineer in electric vehicle repair. “As an engineer, I see a limit as a problem to solve, not a wall,” he said. “I train before work, during lunch, or late at night. What keeps me going is purpose.”</p>



<p>Lunkuse spoke openly about the anxiety she faced ahead of Rio 2016. “I became so focused on qualifying times that I no longer enjoyed it,” she said. “It was only when I took time away that I could see what I was going through and what help I needed — and that’s what got me to Rio 2016.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/04144759/Samsung-Mobile-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Mentoring-session_main4.jpg" alt="Jamila Lunkuse (Uganda), IOC Athlete Role Model at Samsung Mentoring Session. (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)" class="wp-image-178229" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Jamila Lunkuse (Uganda), IOC Athlete Role Model at Samsung Mentoring Session (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)</figcaption></figure>



<p>Daniell described a knee injury in 2022 that kept him out for nearly two years. “What got me through was having something bigger than tennis,” he said. “Everyone fails. Those who last are the ones who learn to pick themselves up when they fall.”</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Beyond the Game: Turning Experience Into Opportunity</strong></h2>



<p>Finally, Makgato asked how the lessons of sport carried into what came next.</p>



<p>Through nonprofit work, Bourhis is building a canoeing ecosystem in Senegal. “We’re building it step by step — and building canoes locally makes the sport more affordable, creates local jobs, and leaves a lasting legacy,” he said. “I want to give the next generation the tools to build their own way.”</p>



<p>Lunkuse, a youth coach and World Aquatics Athletes’ Committee member, urges young athletes to look beyond results. “Results do not define your value,” she said.</p>



<p>Similarly, Daniell — an IOC Athletes’ Commission member and founder of High Impact Athletes, a nonprofit connecting athletes with charities — advised: “Look for a goal beyond personal success. For me, that was what kept pushing me forward.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/04144853/Samsung-Mobile-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Mentoring-session_main5.jpg" alt="Marcus Daniell (New Zealand), Tokyo 2020 Olympic Medalist at Samsung Mentoring Session (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)" class="wp-image-178230" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Marcus Daniell (New Zealand), Tokyo 2020 Olympic Medalist at Samsung Mentoring Session (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Opening Possibilities for the Next Generation</strong></h2>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/04144939/Samsung-Mobile-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Mentoring-session_main6.jpg" alt="Lefa Makgato, CSR Manager at Samsung Electronics Africa, moderated the session (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)" class="wp-image-178231" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Lefa Makgato, CSR Manager at Samsung Electronics Africa, moderated the session. (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)</figcaption></figure>



<p>Makgato closed the session with a story of her own. Growing up in a small township in South Africa, she first encountered a computer at 18 — at one of Samsung’s Solar Powered Internet Schools. She later joined Samsung Electronics Africa, where she now leads CSR programs across the continent, including Solve for Tomorrow, giving the next generation the kind of opportunity she once received. Having seen firsthand how a single opportunity can change a life, she had good reason to stand before the young athletes that day.</p>



<p>“I know firsthand how access to technology and education opens doors you may never have imagined. Where you are today doesn’t determine where you’re going,” she said. “We wanted to encourage young athletes to stay open to new ideas and recognize the potential they have to shape their own futures.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/04145007/Samsung-Mobile-Youth-Olympic-Games-Dakar-2026-Mentoring-session_main7.jpg" alt="(From left) Jamila Lunkuse, Jean-Pierre Bourhis, and Marcus Daniell take a selfie at the Samsung Open Station. (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)" class="wp-image-178232" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ (From left) Jamila Lunkuse, Jean-Pierre Bourhis, and Marcus Daniell took a selfie at the Samsung Open Station. (Copyright: IOC/ Franco Arland)</figcaption></figure>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" />


<ol class="wp-block-footnotes"><li id="4e413af0-326b-42dc-b4d8-97c4dc6e1e1b">IOC Athlete Role Models (ARMs) have been part of the Youth Olympic Games since Singapore 2010. Selected by International Federations for each sport, these experienced Olympians stay on site to speak with young athletes and lead workshops on topics such as career management and injury prevention, helping shape the athletes of tomorrow. <a href="#4e413af0-326b-42dc-b4d8-97c4dc6e1e1b-link" aria-label="Jump to footnote reference 1"><img src="https://s.w.org/images/core/emoji/16.0.1/72x72/21a9.png" alt="↩" class="wp-smiley" style="height: 1em; max-height: 1em;" />︎</a></li></ol> Upgraded Sleep Apnea Feature Now Available on Galaxy Ring Following Regulatory Approvals From US FDA and Korea MFDS https://news.samsung.com/global/upgraded-sleep-apnea-feature-now-available-on-galaxy-ring-following-regulatory-approvals-from-us-fda-and-korea-mfds Tue, 03 Nov 2026 21:00:00 +0000 Samsung Newsroom Mobile Press Releases Galaxy Ring Galaxy Watch9 Samsung Health App Sleep Apnea Feature Wearable https://bit.ly/3Wj2Y8L Samsung Electronics today announced that its updated Sleep Apnea feature is now available on Galaxy Watch and Ring. Following United States Food and Drug Administration (FDA) clearance in July, the updated feature has also received approval from Korea’s Ministry of Food and Drug Safety (MFDS). With this update, Samsung becomes the first company in the world […] <p>Samsung Electronics today announced that its updated Sleep Apnea feature<sup data-fn="fe315b41-51ec-4236-abaa-daa2a1883105" class="fn"><a href="#fe315b41-51ec-4236-abaa-daa2a1883105" id="fe315b41-51ec-4236-abaa-daa2a1883105-link">1</a></sup> is now available on Galaxy Watch and Ring.<sup data-fn="2322e6f7-1ada-4775-8822-c431479d5468" class="fn"><a href="#2322e6f7-1ada-4775-8822-c431479d5468" id="2322e6f7-1ada-4775-8822-c431479d5468-link">2</a></sup> Following United States Food and Drug Administration (FDA) clearance in July, the updated feature has also received approval from Korea’s Ministry of Food and Drug Safety (MFDS). With this update, Samsung becomes the first company in the world to bring sleep apnea risk detection on a smart ring.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/02162459/Samsung-Mobile-Sleep-Apnea-Feature-Galaxy-Ring_Main1.gif" alt="The GIF of Sleep Apnea" class="wp-image-178210" /></figure>



<p>The updated Sleep Apnea feature, available through the Samsung Health Monitor app, marks a significant leap forward from its predecessor. The enhanced algorithm analyzes a user’s vitals during sleep in greater depth and now provides insight into the level of breathing disruptions detected — going beyond the previous sleep apnea risk assessment to deliver more comprehensive, meaningful information that helps users better understand their condition, which can lead to productive conversations with healthcare professionals.</p>



<p>Most notably, Samsung is extending sleep apnea risk detection to a smart ring for the first time. Galaxy Ring’s comfortable, lightweight design makes it easy to wear through the night and convenient for sleep measurement, encouraging nightly wear. With the feature’s low data requirement, users can gain meaningful insight into their sleep health from as little as two nights of monitoring within a 10-day period. By offering the feature on both Galaxy Watch and Galaxy Ring, Samsung lets users monitor their sleep health in the way that best fits their lifestyle.</p>



<p>Samsung’s original Sleep Apnea feature previously received groundbreaking U.S. FDA De Novo authorization in February, 2024 — the first of its kind for a wearable device designed to detect signs of moderate to severe sleep apnea. Building on that clinically validated reliability, the updated feature has now also been approved by Korea’s MFDS last October. Samsung will continue to expand the feature’s availability so more people around the world can detect possible signs of sleep apnea and take proactive steps toward better sleep health. This underscores Samsung’s dedication to accessible preventive care, with sleep health serving as a fundamental pillar.</p>



<p>“Samsung has long worked to make sleep health a simple and seamless part of everyday life,” said Jongmin Choi, Head of Health R&D Group, Mobile eXperience (MX) Business, Samsung Electronics. “This approval from Korea’s MFDS, along with our introduction of the world’s first support for a smart ring, reflects our commitment to accessible innovation. We are giving people the tools to help spot possible early signs of sleep apnea and take charge of their wellbeing.”</p>

This feature is intended to detect signs of moderate to severe sleep apnea in the form of significant breathing disruptions in users 18 years and older, over a two-night monitoring period. It is intended for on-demand use and requires two nights of valid data within a 10-day period. This feature is not intended for users who have previously been diagnosed with sleep apnea. Taking place in Senegal from Oct. 31 to Nov. 13, Dakar 2026 is bringing […] <p><strong>Samsung Electronics</strong>, a Worldwide Olympic and Paralympic Partner, today announced that it is bringing <strong>Galaxy technology and athlete-centered programming</strong> to the <a href="https://www.olympics.com/en/dakar-2026" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Youth Olympic Games Dakar 2026</a> to enrich the experience of young athletes during an important stage in their Olympic journeys.</p>



<p>Taking place in Senegal from Oct. 31 to Nov. 13, Dakar 2026 is bringing together more than <strong>2,700 young athletes </strong>from 207 countries across 25 sports. It also marks a historic milestone as the first Olympic sporting event to be held on the African continent. Additionally, Samsung is drawing on nearly four decades of experience within the Olympic Movement to further strengthen its commitment to the Youth Olympic Games, which dates back to the inaugural edition, Singapore 2010.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/01203732/Samsung-Mobile-Dakar-2026-Victory-Selfie-Galaxy-Technology_main1.jpg" alt="Team Greece athletes hold Galaxy Tab S10 Ultra displaying their country name during the Youth Olympic Games Dakar 2026 Opening Ceremony." class="wp-image-178153" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Team Greece athletes hold Galaxy Tab S10 Ultra displaying their country name during the Youth Olympic Games Dakar 2026 Opening Ceremony. (Copyright: IOC/Ubald Rutar)</figcaption></figure>



<p>“The Youth Olympic Games represent an important stage in a young athlete’s journey — where competition, connection, and personal growth come together,” said Stephanie Choi, Executive Vice President and Head of Global Brand Center at Samsung Electronics. “At Dakar 2026, we are committed to bringing the Games to more people and enriching the experience for the athletes who take part through purposeful Galaxy innovation and programs designed around their needs.”  </p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/01203753/Samsung-Mobile-Dakar-2026-Victory-Selfie-Galaxy-Technology_main2.jpg" alt="Team Korea captures the historic Youth Olympic Games Dakar 2026 Opening Ceremony with Galaxy Z Flip8. <p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Helping Power the Youth Olympic Games: Bringing Galaxy Innovation to Dakar 2026</strong></h2>



<p>Having introduced sports technology integrations at Paris 2024 and Milano Cortina 2026, Samsung is now bringing these innovations to the Youth Olympic Games for the first time.</p>



<p><a href="https://www.samsung.com/us/smartphones/galaxy-s26-ultra/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Galaxy S26 Ultra</a> devices are being incorporated into skateboarding and taekwondo coverage alongside existing broadcast cameras, enabling handheld, close-up, and vertical perspectives that can complement traditional production. Dakar 2026 is also serving as an opportunity to test and learn from these applications as Samsung continues exploring how mobile technology can support future Games experiences — including on the road to the LA28 Games.</p>



<p>The <strong>Olympic Victory Selfie</strong> is making its Youth Olympic Games debut at Dakar 2026, extending a program that has become an iconic expression of athlete celebration at the Olympic and Paralympic Games since its debut at Paris 2024. Centered on connection, emotion, and self-expression, Victory Selfie gives athletes a more personal way to capture and share the moments that define their journey.</p>



<p>Using <strong>Galaxy Z Flip7 Olympic Edition</strong> devices, medalists across disciplines will have the opportunity to commemorate their winning moments as part of Victory Selfie activations around medal ceremonies to create lasting memories of their achievement that are sharable with friends, family, and supporters around the world.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/01203902/Samsung-Mobile-Dakar-2026-Victory-Selfie-Galaxy-Technology_main4.jpg" alt="International Olympic Committee (IOC) President Kirsty Coventry stops by the Samsung Open Station for a Victory Selfie with Team Senegal athletes Woreye Ndiaye (Wushu), Ibrahima Beye (Taekwondo), Naël Lamine Voisin (Swimming), Ndèye Khady Anna Diatta (Beach Wrestling), and Team Korea athletes Jang Yu Jeong (Badminton) and Park Jung Bin (Badminton)." class="wp-image-178157" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> International Olympic Committee (IOC) President Kirsty Coventry stops by the Samsung Open Station for a Victory Selfie with Team Senegal athletes Woreye Ndiaye (Wushu), Ibrahima Beye (Taekwondo), Naël Lamine Voisin (Swimming), Ndèye Khady Anna Diatta (Beach Wrestling), and Team Korea athletes Jang Yu Jeong (Badminton) and Park Jung Bin (Badminton). (Copyright: IOC/Chiara Montesano)</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Supporting Athlete Journeys: Enriching the Games-Time Experience for Young Olympians</strong></h2>



<p>At the <strong>Samsung Open Station</strong> inside the Youth Olympic Village, Samsung is creating a welcoming, relaxed space where athletes can step away from competition, meet peers from around the world, and explore new ways to express themselves through Galaxy technology. Inspired by Dakar’s coastal environment, the space brings together interactive activities designed to help athletes celebrate their individuality, create lasting memories, and reflect on where their journeys may take them next. These activities include:</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/01203841/Samsung-Mobile-Dakar-2026-Victory-Selfie-Galaxy-Technology_main5.jpg" alt="Team USA Women's Rugby player, Chloe Gandy, takes part in an interactive mobile gaming experience at Samsung Open Station at Dakar 2026. " class="wp-image-178156" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Team USA Women’s Rugby player, Chloe Gandy, takes part in an interactive mobile gaming experience at Samsung Open Station at Dakar 2026. (Copyright: IOC/Ubald Rutar)</figcaption></figure>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Victory Talk — </strong>Athletes can capture their Dakar 2026 experience through a short-form video built around casual questions about themselves, their time at the Games, and their aspirations for the future.</li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Victory Wall —</strong> Using Galaxy devices, athletes can transform their photos into personalized Olympic-themed characters and posters, celebrating their individuality and creativity.</li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Victory Photo Zone —</strong> Athletes can recreate the excitement of a medal ceremony moment by capturing and sharing the excitement of standing on the podium.</li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Community Lounge —</strong> A relaxed social space where athletes can connect with one another, play a Dakar-themed mobile skateboarding game, personalize keepsakes, and discover Galaxy Tips designed to help them get more from their devices.</li>
</ul>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li><strong>Mentoring Session: Beyond the Medal —</strong> A mentoring session where Olympians and IOC Athlete Role Models help young athletes shape their lives beyond competition.</li>
</ul>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/01203921/Samsung-Mobile-Dakar-2026-Victory-Selfie-Galaxy-Technology_main6.jpg" alt="Team USA enjoys a visit to Samsung Open Station inside the Dakar 2026 Youth Olympic Village, exploring the immersive experience and innovative Galaxy technology offerings." class="wp-image-178158" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Team USA enjoys a visit to Samsung Open Station inside the Dakar 2026 Youth Olympic Village, exploring the immersive experience and innovative Galaxy technology offerings. (Copyright: IOC/Ubald Rutar)</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Samsung’s Commitment to the Olympic Games: Bringing Dakar 2026 Closer to Local Communities</strong></h2>



<p>Dakar 2026 marks a milestone as both the first Olympic sporting event held on the African continent and as a new chapter in Samsung’s support of the Youth Olympic Games, which dates back to Singapore 2010. Alongside its athlete-facing programs, Samsung is extending the excitement of the Games into communities across Senegal through local media, digital, retail, and consumer activations designed to help more people take part in the excitement of Dakar 2026.</p>



<p><strong>Out-of-home campaigns</strong> will spotlight Dakar 2026 — and Samsung’s programming — across key locations in Dakar and Senegal while Samsung consumer experience zones will bring the excitement of the Youth Olympic Games into local communities. This will give fans opportunities to explore Galaxy technology, take part in interactive sports and AI-powered challenges, and create their own Olympic-inspired moments.</p>



<p>Samsung is also working with domestic partners on their retail initiatives for Dakar 2026, including special Galaxy offers and in-store activations, while a citywide pin program spanning Samsung locations will encourage consumers to explore the Games and participate in Samsung experiences throughout Dakar.</p>



<p>Together, these programs extend Samsung’s commitment to the Olympic Games beyond the competition venues — supporting young athletes at the center of the Games while creating more ways for local fans and communities to experience and celebrate this historic moment.</p>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" />



<h4 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>About Samsung’s Involvement in the Olympic Games</strong></h4>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading">Samsung has been a Worldwide Olympic Partner since the Olympic Winter Games Nagano 1998. For over 25 years, athletes and fans have trusted Samsung’s transformative mobile technology to share the Olympic spirit globally and help to shape the digital future of the Olympic Games. The company’s commitment to the Olympic Movement soon faces its fourth decade of partnership and extends through the LA28 Games. Samsung’s purposeful innovations in the wireless communications and computing equipment category, including equipment that features artificial intelligence, virtual reality, augmented reality, and 5G will help to change the way the world experiences the Olympic Games.</h4>



<p></p>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>About Samsung’s Involvement in the Youth Olympic Games</strong></h4>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading">Samsung has supported the Youth Olympic Games since the inaugural Singapore 2010 Youth Olympic Games, accompanying young athletes as they take on new challenges and begin their Olympic journeys. At Dakar 2026, Samsung continues that commitment by using Galaxy technology and athlete-focused experiences to help the next generation connect, create, and share their stories on the Olympic stage.</h4> Samsung Electronics Announces Third Quarter 2026 Results https://news.samsung.com/global/samsung-electronics-announces-third-quarter-2026-results Thu, 29 Oct 2026 10:10:00 +0000 Samsung Newsroom More Stories Press Releases https://bit.ly/4BqeJud Samsung Electronics today reported financial results for the third quarter ended Sept. 30, 2026. The Company posted KRW 195.3 trillion in consolidated revenue, another all-time quarterly high, representing a quarter-on-quarter (QoQ) increase of 14%. Operating profit reached KRW 107.4 trillion, making Samsung Electronics the world’s first tech company to exceed KRW 100 trillion in quarterly […] <p>Samsung Electronics today reported financial results for the third quarter ended Sept. 30, 2026.</p>



<p>The Company posted KRW 195.3 trillion in consolidated revenue, another all-time quarterly high, representing a quarter-on-quarter (QoQ) increase of 14%. Operating profit reached KRW 107.4 trillion, making Samsung Electronics the world’s first tech company to exceed KRW 100 trillion in quarterly operating profit.</p>



<p>The Device Solutions (DS) Division posted a QoQ sales increase of 15%, with the Memory Business setting an all-time high for quarterly revenue and operating profit, as semiconductor demand is set to remain robust.</p>



<p>The Device eXperience (DX) Division posted a QoQ revenue increase of 8% and recorded an operating loss of KRW 0.8 trillion.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>DS To Sustain Growth by Proactively Meeting Growing Demand for AI Applications</strong></h2>



<p>The DS Division posted KRW 147.1 trillion in consolidated revenue and KRW 106.5 trillion in operating profit for the third quarter.</p>



<p>The Memory Business achieved another record-breaking quarter by managing its product mix, with a focus on AI products, amid capacity constraints. Strong demand momentum for servers led to higher market prices across all applications.</p>



<p>Additionally, the Business experienced continued robust demand, driven by AI applications. It significantly scaled up sales of HBM4 on the back of industry-leading product competitiveness while proactively addressing growing demand for server SSDs.</p>



<p>In Q4 2026, the Memory Business expects industry-wide competition for AI leadership to drive continued AI infrastructure investment. Memory demand growth is projected to revolve around new CPU and GPU platform-related products, while surging QLC SSD demand will be linked to the expansion of personal AI agent services.</p>



<p>The Memory Business will also maintain a sales strategy focused on AI applications in order to solidify market leadership. Leveraging its industry-leading products and unmatched scale, the Business aims to achieve this by further increasing HBM, server DRAM, and SSD sales, while also increasing the supply of high-speed server DDR5 products and QLC SSDs.</p>



<p>In 2027, the Business expects AI infrastructure capital expenditure to reach a historic level as the AI ecosystem expands. Additionally, increasing AI-driven demand will far exceed the industry supply in comparison with 2026. The Business also aims to leverage its superior technology to bolster market leadership and optimize product mix in alignment with market trends and customer needs.</p>



<p>It plans to lead the high-end market and sharpen its AI-driven portfolio while building a stable sales base by securing multi-year contracts and ensuring timely readiness of new products tailored to the requirements of AI applications.</p>



<p>The System LSI Business achieved revenue growth in Q3 2026, driven by the mass production of systems-on-a-chip (SoCs) and image sensors for new models launched in H2. Additionally, the custom SoC business secured projects by establishing new customer partnerships.</p>



<p>In Q4 2026, the Business plans to maintain stable production and sales of flagship SoCs and 200MP image sensors.</p>



<p>In 2027, the System LSI Business aims to expand flagship SoC sales and strengthen partnerships with custom SoC customers. It also plans to accelerate sales of flagship high-resolution image sensors and diversify into non-mobile applications.</p>



<p>The Foundry Business experienced a notable revenue and earnings improvement on HBM base-die sales growth and expanded advanced-node sales, as well as continued order wins centered on 2nm HPC customers, including major hyperscalers.</p>



<p>Looking ahead to Q4 2026, the Foundry Business plans to achieve double-digit revenue growth and continued earnings improvement on new mobile products, mass production ramp-up of 4nm NPUs, and expanded memory product sales.</p>



<p>In 2027, the Business will aim for revenue growth led by 4nm and 2nm advanced nodes based on strong AI and HPC demand. It also expects to strengthen its global supply capabilities with the commencement of operations at its new fab in Taylor, Texas.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>SDC To Build On Growth With Enhanced, Diversified Product Lineup</strong></h2>



<p>Samsung Display Corporation (SDC) posted KRW 8.0 trillion in consolidated revenue and KRW 1.1 trillion in operating profit for the third quarter.</p>



<p>For the small and medium display business, SDC reported improved earnings based on solid demand for high-end mobile products, while the large display business expanded sales volume and revenue on the growth of the gaming monitor market.</p>



<p>In Q4 2026, SDC expects a positive effect from year-end peak seasonal demand and new product launches, but it anticipates a limited improvement in earnings due to external factors.</p>



<p>During the quarter, the small and medium display business will pursue sales expansion by actively responding to customer demand, while the large display business will focus on improving profitability centered on gaming monitors.</p>



<p>In 2027, SDC expects continued market demand weakness amid ongoing uncertainty. For smartphones, the small and medium display business aims to increase the portion of premium products like foldables, and for IT, the business will pursue sales expansion through the ramp-up of the new 8.6G IT line and strengthening of the product lineup. The large display business aims to continue pursuing improved profitability centered on high value-added models.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>MX To Maintain Mobile Leadership by Capitalizing on the High-Growth Premium Market</strong></h2>



<p>The MX and Networks Businesses posted a combined KRW 37.7 trillion in consolidated revenue and reported an operating loss of KRW 0.6 trillion for the third quarter. Revenue increased QoQ and year-on-year (YoY), mainly driven by new flagship launches including foldables. Earnings increased QoQ with an improved flagship sales mix, but declined YoY due to cost pressures.</p>



<p>In Q4 2026, the MX Business plans to drive year-end peak-season sales growth centered on flagships including the Galaxy S26 series, Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra, and Z Fold8, as well as new ecosystem products. It will also seek to mitigate profitability risks through efficiency improvements across all business processes.</p>



<p>In 2027, the MX Business aims to maintain its mobile leadership by focusing on three strategic priorities. First, it will improve its product mix, centered on flagships with differentiated experiences by customer segment. Second, it will enhance the desirability and deliver tangible value of products through hardware and form-factor innovation. Third, it will minimize declines in profit with agile market responses and operational efficiency initiatives.</p>



<p>In Q3 2026, the Networks Business saw a decline in earnings QoQ and YoY due to lower North American and high-margin software sales. In Q4 2026, the Business aims to secure profitability through revenue growth in North America, Korea, and Japan. In 2027, the Business plans to drive profitable growth by securing new orders amid telco investment headwinds.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>VD/DA Businesses and Harman To Extend AI-Based Product Innovation</strong></h2>



<p>The Visual Display (VD) and Digital Appliances (DA) Businesses posted a combined KRW 13.9 trillion in consolidated revenue and reported an operating loss of KRW 0.2 trillion for the third quarter.</p>



<p>The VD Business saw revenue decline due to the conclusion of major sporting events, while earnings decreased due to increased costs. In Q4 2026, the Business aims to capture peak season demand through strengthened channel partnerships and differentiated marketing, while expanding sales of premium products in order to offset the impact of rising costs. In 2027, while overall TV demand is expected to remain flat or decline slightly, the Business will focus on securing profitable growth engines by delivering differentiated TV experiences, as well as strengthening the service and platform businesses.</p>



<p>Meanwhile, the DA Business’ earnings declined due to higher costs related to raw materials and logistics, despite stronger domestic marketing efforts and growth in the B2B and subscription businesses. In Q4 2026, the Business intends to improve profitability with a higher premium mix, and in 2027, it aims to secure profitability by expanding sales of innovative AI products and growing its high-margin B2B businesses.</p>



<p>Harman posted improved earnings on expanded audio and automotive sales, with a boost from non-recurring U.S. tariff refunds. Looking ahead, the Business will seek to maintain stable growth momentum by developing new automotive business and extending audio leadership through portfolio enhancements.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Shareholder Return: Q3 Dividend Details and Total Payout</strong></h2>



<p>The third-quarter cash dividend amounts to KRW 4,601 per common share and KRW 4,601 per preferred share, totaling KRW 30 trillion.</p>



<p>Of the estimated KRW 90~110 trillion in total shareholder returns for 2026, and with KRW 35 trillion already planned or completed, the company anticipates the remaining funds to be KRW 55~75 trillion.</p>



<p>The return of the remaining funds will include a substantial share repurchase and cancellation program, along with cash dividends.</p>



<p>The Company recognizes the growing market interest in the price gap between common and preferred shares, and is committed to actively considering and adjusting the proportion of preferred shares in our share repurchase and cancellation program based on market conditions.</p>



<p>The next shareholder return policy, along with the final amount of shareholder returns and the specific execution plans for the remaining funds for 2026, will be finalized at the Board meeting scheduled for late January 2027, with an announcement to follow.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Third Quarter 2026 Capital Expenditures</strong></h2>



<p>In the third quarter, Samsung Electronics’ capital expenditure reached KRW 29 trillion, an increase of KRW 12.2 trillion quarter-on-quarter (QoQ) and KRW 19.8 trillion year-on-year (YoY). Capital expenditure consisted of KRW 27.2 trillion spent in the Device Solutions (DS) Division and KRW 1.1 trillion in Samsung Display Corporation (SDC). The cumulative total for the January-September period is KRW 56.9 trillion, up KRW 24.7 trillion YoY, with KRW 52.8 trillion allocated to the DS Division and KRW 2.4 trillion to SDC.</p>



<p>The full-year capital expenditure for 2026 is expected at approximately KRW 84.4 trillion, including KRW 76.8 trillion allocated to the DS Division and KRW 3.9 trillion to SDC.</p>



<p>For the DS Division, investments are planned for new fabs and other infrastructure to meet strong AI and HPC demand while ensuring readiness for the mid-to long term.
