Combining a clip-on design with rich […] <p>Earbuds have become an everyday essential for listening to music, taking calls, exercising, and working. With all-day use increasingly common, comfort and awareness of one’s surroundings are now just as important as sound quality.</p>



<p>To meet these needs, Samsung Electronics has launched Galaxy Buds On, its first open-ear earbuds. Combining a clip-on design with rich sound and AI-powered features, Galaxy Buds On offers greater freedom and flexibility.</p>



<p>Samsung Newsroom put Galaxy Buds On to the test to see how they perform in everyday situations.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading">🕢 07:30 <br>Comfort and Awareness on Every Run</h2>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/26121640/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main1.jpg" alt="Galaxy Buds On" class="wp-image-178009" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Galaxy Buds On</figcaption></figure>



<p>Galaxy Buds On’s clip-on design rests lightly on the ears for a comfortable fit. Even with vigorous head movements, the earbuds stay securely in place.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-video"><video playsinline autoplay controls loop muted src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/27174041/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main2-1.mp4"></video><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Users can adjust the band’s angle to fit their ear shape.</figcaption></figure>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="495" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/26121727/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main3.jpg" alt="Galaxy Buds On features an ergonomic design with speaker and battery placement optimized through data analysis and simulations involving a wide range of ear shapes." class="wp-image-178011" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Galaxy Buds On features an ergonomic design with speaker and battery placement optimized through data analysis and simulations involving a wide range of ear shapes.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Before a run, users can explore routes while warming up without taking out their smartphones. Saying “Hey Google, let’s talk Live” launches Gemini Live, allowing them to ask for running route recommendations and details such as distances and key features.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="650" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/26154424/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main4.jpg" alt="Galaxy Buds On offers hands-free access to Gemini Live when paired with a Galaxy AI-enabled smartphone." class="wp-image-178045" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Galaxy Buds On offers hands-free access to Gemini Live when paired with a Galaxy AI-enabled smartphone.</figcaption></figure>



<p>A tap on the back of an earbud starts music playback, and TwinBoost speaker delivers enhanced bass compared to previous Galaxy Buds models for richer sound.</p>



<p>The clip-on design lets users hear passing bicycles and runners approaching from behind while listening to music, helping them stay aware of their surroundings.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/26121824/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main5.jpg" alt="Users can run safely and comfortably with Galaxy Buds On." class="wp-image-178013" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Users can run safely and comfortably with Galaxy Buds On.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Voice commands offer another way to control music playback without touching the earbuds. Users can say “Next song” or “Previous song” to switch tracks or “Volume up” and “Volume down” to adjust the volume.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-video"><video playsinline autoplay controls loop muted src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/27174131/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main6-1.mp4"></video><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Galaxy Buds On offers hands-free access to Gemini Live when paired with a Galaxy AI-enabled smartphone.</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading">🕙 10:00 <br>A Smart, Hands-Free Work Companion</h2>



<p>Galaxy Buds On also proves useful in shared workspaces, thanks to Audio Leakage Reduction<sup data-fn="c9f90d03-92f7-46b1-95ec-9416f7fec9f9" class="fn"><a href="#c9f90d03-92f7-46b1-95ec-9416f7fec9f9" id="c9f90d03-92f7-46b1-95ec-9416f7fec9f9-link">1</a></sup> technology that minimizes sound escaping from the earbuds.</p>



<p>After a meeting, Quick Voice Memo<sup data-fn="e0fa998d-73ff-4053-aa73-9387366d1f03" class="fn"><a href="#e0fa998d-73ff-4053-aa73-9387366d1f03" id="e0fa998d-73ff-4053-aa73-9387366d1f03-link">2</a></sup> helps users organize their to-do lists. Once user speaks after pressing and holding the back of an earbud, recording starts immediately. Later, users can ask Bixby about their tasks and hear the details aloud, eliminating the need to search through saved recordings.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="650" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/26122725/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main7.jpg" alt=" Quick Voice Memo" class="wp-image-178014" /></figure>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="650" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/26122736/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main8.jpg" alt="Quick Voice Memo" class="wp-image-178015" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Quick Voice Memo</figcaption></figure>



<p>Head Gestures feature comes in handy when users have their hands full. They can nod to answer incoming calls or shake their heads to decline them. Simple head movements also let users check message notifications and dismiss alarms.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-video"><video playsinline autoplay controls loop muted src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/27174205/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main9-1.mp4"></video><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Head Gestures</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading">🕡 18:30 <br>Clear Calls, Even on Noisy Commutes</h2>



<p>Galaxy Buds On remains comfortable even after hours of wear, without putting pressure on the ears.</p>



<p>The earbuds also maintain call clarity in noisy environments, such as crowded subway stations.</p>



<p>Intelligent Clear Call makes this possible. Three microphones, a bone conduction-based Voice Pickup Unit Sensor, and a machine learning-based solution work together to isolate the user’s voice and reduce background noise.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/26122922/Samsung-Mobile-Galaxy-Buds-On-Gemini-Live-Clip-On-Design_Main10.jpg" alt="Galaxy Buds On features Intelligent Clear Call." class="wp-image-178017" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><strong>▲</strong> Galaxy Buds On features Intelligent Clear Call.</figcaption></figure>



<p>From morning runs to the journey home, Galaxy Buds On fits naturally into the rhythm of the day. Its clip-on design, Voice Command, and AI-powered features make everyday tasks easier to manage.</p>



<p>Galaxy Buds On lets users enjoy their audio without losing touch with the world around them.</p>

For 80 years, JBL has evolved alongside changing listening habits while remaining deeply connected to music and culture. Meanwhile, Bowers & Wilkins has spent 60 years pursuing exceptional sound through precision engineering, craftsmanship, and an uncompromising commitment to reproducing the original performance. […] <p>From concerts and cars to homes and headphones, high-quality sound enriches everyday experiences. For 80 years, JBL has evolved alongside changing listening habits while remaining deeply connected to music and culture. Meanwhile, Bowers & Wilkins has spent 60 years pursuing exceptional sound through precision engineering, craftsmanship, and an uncompromising commitment to reproducing the original performance.</p>



<p>Both brands are part of HARMAN, a wholly owned subsidiary of Samsung Electronics. In 2026, the companies mark three milestones — JBL’s 80th anniversary, Bowers & Wilkins’ 60th anniversary, and 10 years of the Samsung-HARMAN partnership.</p>



<p>Samsung Newsroom spoke with Dave Rogers, President of the Lifestyle Division, and Armin Prommersberger, Chief Technology Officer, both at HARMAN, about the values that have shaped the two audio brands, the evolution of the Samsung-HARMAN partnership, and what lies ahead.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/23173007/Samsung-More-Products-HARMAN-JBL-Sound-That-Connects-Interveiw_Main1.jpg" alt="(From left) Armin Prommersberger and Dave Rogers" class="wp-image-177980" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ (From left) Armin Prommersberger and Dave Rogers</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>A Legacy of Pioneering Spirit and Innovation</strong></h2>



<p><strong>Q. What has allowed JBL and Bowers & Wilkins to remain relevant across generations?</strong><strong></strong></p>



<p><strong>Rogers: </strong>JBL has never assumed that what worked yesterday would be enough for tomorrow. The brand has expanded beyond cinemas and studios into festivals, cars, homes, headphones, and portable speakers.</p>



<p>Technology, listening habits, and culture have changed, but the reason people care about sound remains the same. Sound evokes emotion, creates memories, and brings people together.</p>



<p>The journey from the JBL 4310 studio monitor to the JBL L100 loudspeaker captures this well. The 4310 was developed for professionals whose work demanded critical listening. JBL then carried that professional audio DNA into the L100, bringing professional-grade sound into the home. The L100 went on to become the brand’s best-selling loudspeaker. More than a technological evolution, this transition reflected JBL’s ability to connect the people who shape culture with those who live it.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="491" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/23180604/Samsung-More-Products-HARMAN-JBL-Sound-That-Connects-Interveiw_Main2.jpg" alt="(From left) JBL 4310 studio monitor and JBL L100 Classic 80" class="wp-image-177996" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ (From left) JBL 4310 studio monitor and JBL L100 Classic 80</figcaption></figure>



<p>Bowers & Wilkins has followed a different path from JBL. Designed for accuracy, its 801 loudspeaker became a reference monitor for engineers at Abbey Road Studios — the recording studio that has worked with some of the world’s most celebrated artists — reflecting the brand’s commitment to acoustic precision and fidelity to the original performance.</p>



<p>Different personalities, but the same refusal to compromise on what matters.</p>



<p></p>



<p><strong>Q. What is one core value of JBL and Bowers & Wilkins that should never change?</strong></p>



<p><strong>Rogers: </strong>Our obsession with quality and the experiences both brands deliver. Products and technology will change, but how we make people feel — that’s the standard.</p>



<p><strong>Prommersberger: </strong>Curiosity. The moment an engineer says, “That’s good enough,” we have a problem. Fortunately, we have quite a few people at HARMAN who find “good enough” deeply irritating.</p>



<p>Bowers & Wilkins’ Nautilus loudspeaker is a great example. The brand had already made significant progress in reducing unwanted vibration with its Matrix cabinet technology, but John Bowers and his research team questioned whether the cabinet itself was part of the problem. That question led to five years of research and ultimately to the development of Nautilus, featuring a tapered-tube design to absorb unwanted sound from the back of the drive units.</p>



<p>Good engineering is not only about solving problems. Sometimes, it is about becoming uncomfortable with the solutions that have already made you successful.</p>



<p></p>



<p><strong>Q. And what must continue to change?</strong></p>



<p><strong>Prommersberger: </strong>Technology. People’s expectations don’t stand still, and we’re moving toward experiences that understand context, adapt intelligently, and become more personal.</p>



<p>Today, we still do a lot of work for our devices — choosing modes, opening apps, and adjusting settings. With AI and greater connectivity, technology can better understand us and adapt more naturally to our needs.</p>



<p>The breakthrough will not be that our devices can do more. It will be that we have to manage them less.<strong></strong></p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Complementary Strengths, Shared Ambition</strong></h2>



<p><strong>Q. </strong><strong>What have</strong><strong> Samsung and HARMAN </strong><strong>achieved and learned over</strong><strong> 10 years </strong><strong>of</strong><strong> partnership?</strong></p>



<p><strong>Rogers: </strong>One of the earliest examples was bringing HARMAN’s audio expertise into the Galaxy experience through AKG, a HARMAN company. We combined our knowledge of sound with Samsung’s capabilities in mobile technology, design, and scale to enhance products people already use every day.</p>



<p>We then applied this approach more broadly through the Automotive Digital Cockpit, combining Samsung’s strengths in displays and connected ecosystems with HARMAN’s automotive and in-cabin expertise. The ambition was no longer simply to bring better technology into cars but to see them as part of people’s connected lives.</p>



<p>We’ve learned how to turn our differences into strengths.<strong></strong></p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/23173106/Samsung-More-Products-HARMAN-JBL-Sound-That-Connects-Interveiw_Main3.jpg" alt="Dave Rogers" class="wp-image-177982" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Dave Rogers</figcaption></figure>



<p>Throughout our journey together, we’ve also learned to think bigger and faster. Samsung has an extraordinary ability to turn ideas into technology that touches people’s lives on an enormous scale. That mindset challenges us — in a good way.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>The Future of Sound That Moves People</strong></h2>



<p><strong>Q. Samsung and HARMAN share a vision for connected experiences. What could that look like throughout someone’s day?</strong></p>



<p><strong>Prommersberger:</strong> Imagine a typical day. You wake up, and your Galaxy devices are ready to help you start the day. When you leave home and get into your car, the vehicle can adapt to your journey and the preferences you’ve chosen to share.</p>



<p>Your audio experience continues naturally. Information is prioritized based on the situation while you’re driving, and the cabin adjusts to the people inside. On the way home, your car and home can work together to make the transition feel almost seamless.</p>



<p>A truly connected day should have fewer seams in it. Users shouldn’t have to think about which app to open or which setting to change. They should simply feel that technology understands the flow of their day and gets out of the way.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/23173126/Samsung-More-Products-HARMAN-JBL-Sound-That-Connects-Interveiw_Main4.jpg" alt="Armin Prommersberger" class="wp-image-177983" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ Armin Prommersberger</figcaption></figure>



<p><strong>Q. What excites you most about the next 10 years?</strong><strong></strong></p>



<p><strong>Prommersberger: </strong>I think technology is going to understand us much better — our environment, preferences, activities, and needs. That creates new possibilities for sound, mobility, and connected experiences.</p>



<p>Technology should become more intelligent so the experience can become more human. If we get that right, people won’t notice the complexity underneath. They’ll simply feel the difference.</p>



<p><strong>Rogers: </strong>Eighty years from now, people may not remember every technology we launched. But they’ll remember the experiences those technologies made possible — the concert they felt part of, the song they couldn’t stop playing, the drive they’ll never forget, and the people they shared those moments with. Technology changes quickly, but human emotion doesn’t. Our opportunity is to keep bringing those two things together.<a id="_msocom_1"></a></p>



<figure class="wp-block-embed is-type-video is-provider-youtube wp-block-embed-youtube wp-embed-aspect-16-9 wp-has-aspect-ratio"><div class="wp-block-embed__wrapper">

</div></figure> Inside JBL Playback Gallery: 80 Years of Sound Innovation https://news.samsung.com/global/inside-jbl-playback-gallery-80-years-of-sound-innovation Fri, 23 Oct 2026 08:00:00 +0000 Samsung Newsroom More Products 80 Years of JBL Bowers & Wilkins HARMAN JBL JBL Playback Gallery https://bit.ly/4zMEB1F To celebrate its 80th anniversary, JBL will host the JBL Playback Gallery at Project Space Line in Seoul from October 24 to 31. The exhibition traces the globally renowned audio brand’s evolution through eight decades of innovation, inviting visitors to explore its history firsthand. Throughout the year, HARMAN has showcased JBL’s iconic products from different […] <p>To celebrate its 80th anniversary, JBL will host the JBL Playback Gallery at Project Space Line in Seoul from October 24 to 31. The exhibition traces the globally renowned audio brand’s evolution through eight decades of innovation, inviting visitors to explore its history firsthand.</p>



<p>Throughout the year, HARMAN has showcased JBL’s iconic products from different eras in major cities — including Los Angeles, Amsterdam, Vienna, Munich, Nagoya, and Tokyo. The Seoul exhibition marks the final stop on the global tour, with a specially curated experience that reflects the city’s cultural identity and changing lifestyles over the decades.</p>



<p>Spanning three floors, the JBL Playback Gallery brings together 80 years of JBL history and its latest products, alongside an exhibition dedicated to the 60th anniversary of premium audio brand Bowers & Wilkins. Samsung Newsroom got an exclusive preview ahead of the opening.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="853" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22182339/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main1.jpg" alt="The JBL Playback Gallery commemorates JBL’s 80th anniversary and Bowers & Wilkins’ 60th anniversary." class="wp-image-177933" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The JBL Playback Gallery commemorates JBL’s 80th anniversary and Bowers & Wilkins’ 60th anniversary.</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>JBL Heritage: A Journey Through 80 Years of Sound Heritage</strong></h2>



<p>At the heart of the exhibition was the “JBL Heritage” section on the second floor. Under the theme “A Legacy of Sound,” the space chronicled eight decades of audio innovation through signature JBL products displayed in recreated living rooms from the 1920s, 1950s, and 1970s — capturing the atmosphere of each era.</p>



<p>The 1920s living room featured a 1929 Jackson-Bell radio equipped with speakers produced by Lansing Manufacturing, a company founded in Los Angeles in 1927 by JBL founder James B. Lansing. The model offered a glimpse into the early work that laid the foundation for the brand.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="400" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22182400/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main2.jpg" alt="A Jackson-Bell radio in the 1920s living room" class="wp-image-177934" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ A Jackson-Bell radio in the 1920s living room</figcaption></figure>



<p>In the 1950s living room, the JBL Paragon blended into its surroundings like a piece of furniture. Launched in 1957, the home stereo speaker system housed left and right speakers in a single cabinet. Each unit required approximately 100 hours of woodworking and featured a distinctive curved wooden design.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22182421/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main3.jpg" alt="The JBL Paragon in the 1950s living room" class="wp-image-177935" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The JBL Paragon in the 1950s living room</figcaption></figure>



<p>Complete with a turntable and vinyl records, the 1970s living room illustrated how professional audio technology made its way into homes. On display was the JBL L100 Century, a home loudspeaker launched in 1970 based on the 4310 studio monitor introduced in 1968. It went on to become one of JBL’s most successful home loudspeakers.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="553" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22182505/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main4.jpg" alt="The JBL L100 Century in the 1970s living room" class="wp-image-177936" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The JBL L100 Century in the 1970s living room</figcaption></figure>



<p>Beyond the period-inspired settings, “Playback Gallery: A Story of JBL Through the Ages” charted major milestones — from James B. Lansing’s early speaker designs and the founding of JBL to technological advances in recording studios, cinemas, and concert venues. The speakers on display and their stories revealed how the brand’s innovations have contributed to the evolution of music over the past 80 years.</p>

<h4 class="wp-block-heading has-text-align-center">▲ The “Playback Gallery” showcases key moments and products from JBL’s 80-year history.</h4>



<p>The “Pro to Home” section explored how JBL’s professional audio technologies developed for cinemas and recording studios have evolved for home use. Among the highlights was the Summit K2, the latest model in a high-end loudspeaker lineage dating back to the introduction of Project K2 in 1989. Building on the series’ pursuit of exceptional home audio performance, the Summit K2 combines advanced acoustic technologies with powerful dynamics and nuanced musical detail.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="390" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22182913/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main7.jpg" alt="The “Pro to Home” section explored how JBL’s professional audio technologies developed for cinemas and recording studios have evolved for home use. " class="wp-image-177941" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The “Pro to Home” exhibit</figcaption></figure>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22182933/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main8.jpg" alt="Among the highlights was the Summit K2, the latest model in a high-end loudspeaker lineage dating back to the introduction of Project K2 in 1989." class="wp-image-177942" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The JBL Summit K2</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>JBL Today: Sound in Everyday Life</strong></h2>



<p>While the second floor explored JBL’s history, the first floor focused on the brand today. Positioned at the entrance was the <strong>L100 Classic 80</strong>, a limited-edition model that combines the iconic design of the original 1970s L100 with modern technology. Produced in just 800 pairs worldwide to celebrate JBL’s 80th anniversary, each set features a commemorative plaque engraved with the signature of Chief Systems Engineer Chris Hagen and a unique serial number.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="724" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22183011/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main9.jpg" alt="Positioned at the entrance was the L100 Classic 80, a limited-edition model that combines the iconic design of the original 1970s L100 with modern technology." class="wp-image-177943" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The JBL L100 Classic 80</figcaption></figure>



<p>This year also marks a decade since Samsung Electronics announced its acquisition of HARMAN, JBL’s parent company. The portable speaker and headphone zones reflected how the partnership has brought JBL products into more aspects of everyday life.</p>



<p>Inspired by Seoul’s contrasting day and night scenes, the space featured two distinct zones. “<strong>Seoul by Night</strong>” evoked an urban campsite against the backdrop of N Seoul Tower after dark, with Bluetooth speakers and <strong>PartyBox</strong> models on display. Meanwhile, “<strong>Seoul by Day</strong>” recreated a city street in daylight, featuring JBL headphones and earbuds designed for everyday listening.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="645" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22183036/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main10.jpg" alt="“Seoul by Night” evoked an urban campsite against the backdrop of N Seoul Tower after dark, with Bluetooth speakers and PartyBox models on display." class="wp-image-177944" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The “Seoul by Night” zone</figcaption></figure>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="669" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22183049/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main11.jpg" alt="“Seoul by Day” recreated a city street in daylight, featuring JBL headphones and earbuds designed for everyday listening." class="wp-image-177945" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The “Seoul by Day” zone</figcaption></figure>



<p>An art wall created in collaboration with Korean artist DAWN added a local touch. The artwork reimagined JBL’s 80th-anniversary campaign logo using traditional Korean patterns, bronze bells, and the colors of “dancheong,” a decorative painting style found on traditional Korean architecture.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="647" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22183127/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main12.jpg" alt="An art wall created in collaboration with DAWN incorporates traditional Korean motifs." class="wp-image-177946" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ An art wall created in collaboration with DAWN incorporates traditional Korean motifs.</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Bowers & Wilkins: Six Decades of True Sound</strong></h2>



<p>On the third floor, the exhibition concluded with “60 Years of True Sound,” a section dedicated to Bowers & Wilkins’ 60th anniversary. Founded in the United Kingdom in 1966, the premium audio brand joined HARMAN’s portfolio following its acquisition of Masimo’s audio business last year.</p>



<p>The display introduced founder John Bowers’ “True Sound<strong>”</strong> philosophy and followed the brand’s evolution through signature products — from its first speaker, the P2H, to the iconic Nautilus. At the center of the space stood five models from the recently unveiled flagship <strong>800 Series Diamond D5</strong> lineup.</p>



<p>The exhibition also highlighted Bowers & Wilkins’ longstanding relationship with Abbey Road Studios, where numerous Grammy Award-winning recordings have been produced. Since the original 801 was introduced at the studio in 1979, successive generations of the 800 Series have served as reference monitors for engineers and producers working on music recordings and film soundtracks. Personal audio products, including wireless headphones, were also on display.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="682" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22183147/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main13.jpg" alt="On the third floor, the exhibition concluded with “60 Years of True Sound,” a section dedicated to Bowers & Wilkins’ 60th anniversary." class="wp-image-177947" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The “60 Years of True Sound” exhibition</figcaption></figure>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="485" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/22183201/Samsung-More-Products-JBL-Playback-Gallery-Sketch_main14.jpg" alt="At the center of the space stood five models from the recently unveiled flagship 800 Series Diamond D5 lineup." class="wp-image-177948" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The flagship Bowers & Wilkins 800 Series Diamond D5 loudspeakers</figcaption></figure>



<p>The preview offered just a glimpse of what awaits visitors when the exhibition opens. Starting October 24, guided tours will introduce visitors to JBL’s 80-year history, while product demonstrations will offer a chance to hear its signature sound firsthand.</p>



<p>JBL’s 80-year history reflects how the changing ways people create and enjoy music. Technologies developed for recording studios and live venues have found their way into homes and everyday life through loudspeakers and portable audio products. Bringing together iconic products from different eras and the latest models, the JBL Playback Gallery invites visitors to discover the brand’s enduring pursuit of better sound.</p> [Infographic] 80 Years of JBL: From Studios and Stages to Everyday Life https://news.samsung.com/global/infographic-80-years-of-jbl-from-studios-and-stages-to-everyday-life Wed, 21 Oct 2026 08:00:00 +0000 Samsung Newsroom More Products 80 Years of JBL HARMAN infographic JBL https://bit.ly/3UwN75Q The devices people use to listen to music and the ways they enjoy it have changed over time. How has JBL, the professional audio brand of HARMAN, a Samsung subsidiary, maintained its appeal amid these shifts?</p>



<p>As JBL celebrates its 80th anniversary in 2026, the company’s history is closely intertwined with how people create and enjoy music. Audio technologies refined in recording studios and live venues have made their way into everyday life, while JBL’s products have evolved alongside changing listening habits. Decades of innovation and experience have helped define JBL’s signature sound.</p>

<p>Samsung Week is Samsung’s largest annual promotion for customers around the world. Now in its seventh year, the event is being held under the theme “Discover New Ways to Live Smarter.”</p>



<p>During Samsung Week 2026, the company is offering special purchase benefits on Samsung.com and showcasing new experiences enabled by its latest AI products.<sup data-fn="8b72f320-768c-4067-9484-fba47c9eccbc" class="fn"><a href="#8b72f320-768c-4067-9484-fba47c9eccbc" id="8b72f320-768c-4067-9484-fba47c9eccbc-link">1</a></sup></p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>More Benefits, More Choices on Samsung.com</strong></h2>



<p>For Samsung Week 2026, Samsung has enhanced its online offers and premium services.<sup data-fn="6d03f0fa-913c-4644-a5a7-2a626859b2df" class="fn"><a href="#6d03f0fa-913c-4644-a5a7-2a626859b2df" id="6d03f0fa-913c-4644-a5a7-2a626859b2df-link">2</a></sup></p>



<p>Customers can get more out of their Samsung Rewards Points on Samsung.com throughout the event. Samsung Rewards Points can be applied toward purchases of devices and accessories as well as digital content and services including Galaxy Store, Samsung Art Store, and Samsung Food.</p>



<p>Based on devices registered to their Samsung Account, customers can receive personalized product recommendations. Samsung.com may recommend Bespoke AI appliances that connect through SmartThings for a more integrated home experience, or Galaxy Watch models that support health and fitness goals through Samsung Health. Customers can also easily access Samsung Care+, a professional product care service, and trade-in options.</p>



<p>The latest mobile products are also available in Samsung.com-exclusive colors including Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra in Green Shadow, Galaxy Z Fold8 in Pistachio, and Galaxy Z Flip8 in Mint.</p>



<p>In addition, purchases of the new Galaxy Z series, Galaxy S26 FE, or Galaxy Tab S12 series come with a six-month subscription to Google AI Pro.</p>



<p>Meanwhile, Samsung is promoting Samsung Week 2026 through large digital billboards at iconic landmarks around the world, including Times Square in New York and Piccadilly Circus in London.</p>

<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Smarter Experiences for Work, Wellness, and Home</strong></h2>



<p>Throughout Samsung Week 2026, Samsung is highlighting products featuring AI-powered innovation and enhanced services designed to make everyday life more convenient across productivity, health, cooking, and entertainment.<sup data-fn="1e7e3f58-27e1-45e2-b5f4-05e69a39c8db" class="fn"><a href="#1e7e3f58-27e1-45e2-b5f4-05e69a39c8db" id="1e7e3f58-27e1-45e2-b5f4-05e69a39c8db-link">3</a></sup></p>



<p>Galaxy Z Fold8 Ultra makes it easier to work efficiently using Multi Window on a large screen and, when paired with a Smart Monitor, helps users stay focused wherever they are.</p>



<p>Together, Galaxy Watch Ultra2 and Galaxy S26 Ultra support healthier lifestyles with detailed workout data and health insights, while Galaxy Buds4 Pro adds to the enjoyment of exercise with a comfortable fit.</p>



<p>In the kitchen, the Bespoke AI Refrigerator, Bespoke AI Oven, and Galaxy Z Fold8 create a smarter cooking experience — from ingredient management and recipe recommendations to automated cooking settings.</p>



<p>Samsung Micro RGB TV and soundbars deliver immersive viewing and gaming, while the Bespoke AI Laundry Combo simplifies laundry by handling everything from washing to drying.</p>



<p>For more details about Samsung Week 2026 in each region, visit Samsung.com.</p>

Looking ahead, the company aims to push creative boundaries by bringing artistic imagination and technology together in new ways.</p> Tailored for Every Home: Samsung “The Brief” Goes Global With AI-Powered Home Appliances https://news.samsung.com/global/tailored-for-every-home-samsung-the-brief-goes-global-with-ai-powered-home-appliances Tue, 20 Oct 2026 08:00:31 +0000 Samsung Newsroom Home Appliances AI Home Appliances Bespoke AI Home Companion HVAC SmartThings The Brief https://bit.ly/4Bf3Qv0 Samsung Electronics hosted “The Brief,” a series of home appliance tech seminars across five regions: the US, Europe, Latin America, Southwest Asia, and Southeast Asia. The event showcased AI-powered home appliance experiences reflecting each region’s living environment and consumer needs. Across the five regions, Samsung highlighted AI appliance technologies and how they are applied in […] <p>Samsung Electronics hosted “The Brief,” a series of home appliance tech seminars across five regions: the US, Europe, Latin America, Southwest Asia, and Southeast Asia. The event showcased AI-powered home appliance experiences reflecting each region’s living environment and consumer needs.</p>



<p>Across the five regions, Samsung highlighted AI appliance technologies and how they are applied in everyday life to reflect local lifestyles: kitchen-centered AI homes in North America; energy-efficient built-in AI appliances in Europe; SmartThings-connected AI homes in Latin America; climate- and family-conscious solutions in Southwest Asia; and appliances for homes and businesses in Southeast Asia.</p>



<p>The events brought together about 270 media representatives and influencers from 23 countries. Through hands-on experiences with a range of products, attendees discovered how AI appliances can serve as “Home Companions,” reducing the burden of household chores, managing energy consumption, and looking after the everyday needs of family members.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>US: Making Everyday Life at Home More Convenient With AI Appliances</strong></h2>



<p>“The Brief New York,” held April 15–16 (local time) at a Samsung showroom in New Jersey, focused on how AI appliances can make household chores less demanding and improve quality of life. Samsung demonstrated AI features in refrigerators and ovens that make everyday kitchen tasks easier, from managing ingredients to preparing meals.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/19160856/Samsung-Corporate-The-Brief-Global-with-AI-Powered-Home-Appliances_Main1.jpg" alt=" At "The Brief New York" in New Jersey, Samsung showcased AI features such as "AI Vision" and "AI Pro Cooking" that help make everyday household tasks easier." class="wp-image-177835" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ At “The Brief New York” in New Jersey, Samsung showcased AI features such as “AI Vision” and “AI Pro Cooking” that help make everyday household tasks easier.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Attendees saw how the “AI Vision” feature of the Bespoke AI Refrigerator Family Hub recognizes not only food ingredients but also labels on containers, automatically adding items to the Food List. The demonstration showed how AI helps users easily keep track of what is in their refrigerator and avoid unnecessary duplicate purchases.</p>



<p>The “AI Pro Cooking” feature of the Bespoke AI Oven also drew attention. Its internal camera recognizes ingredients, recommends optimal cooking settings, and monitors changes in food color to notify users as their meals cook. Together, these capabilities demonstrated how AI can take the hassle out of cooking.</p>



<p>Samsung also presented a refrigerator lineup designed around the needs of North American consumers, who value ample food storage, hygiene, and efficient use of space, underscoring its ability to deliver AI appliances tailored to local needs.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Europe: Helping Consumers Save Energy With High-Efficiency Built-In AI Appliances</strong></h2>



<p>Energy efficiency took center stage at “The Brief Milan,” held April 21 in Milan, Italy, where Samsung presented a range of high-efficiency AI appliances for European consumers.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/19161356/Samsung-Corporate-The-Brief-Global-with-AI-Powered-Home-Appliances_Main2.jpg" alt="At "The Brief Milan" in Milan, Italy, Samsung showcased a lineup of energy-efficient built-in appliances, offering solutions suited to European living environments." class="wp-image-177836" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ At “The Brief Milan” in Milan, Italy, Samsung showcased a lineup of energy-efficient built-in appliances, offering solutions suited to European living environments.</figcaption></figure>



<p>The “Bespoke AI Washer” drew attention for reducing energy use by an additional 65% compared with Europe’s Class A energy efficiency threshold, while the “Bespoke AI Dishwasher” cuts energy use by up to 20% against the same benchmark. Samsung highlighted how these advanced energy-saving technologies can help consumers reduce their energy consumption while offering the added benefits of AI-powered appliances.</p>



<p>In the kitchen, Samsung presented the Extractor Induction Hob, the built-in Bespoke AI Dishwasher, and a refrigerator with “AI Precise Cooling,” which helps minimize internal temperature fluctuations.</p>



<p>Together, these innovations illustrated Samsung’s vision for an AI home optimized for European living environments, combining energy-saving technologies, appliances that blend seamlessly into living spaces, and connected experiences powered by SmartThings.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Latin America: Bringing AI Homes Together Through SmartThings Connectivity</strong></h2>



<p>In Latin America, SmartThings connectivity was a central theme, bringing multiple appliances together into a unified AI home ecosystem. Events were held on August 19 in São Paulo, Brazil, and September 9 in Mexico City, Mexico.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="491" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/19162213/Samsung-Corporate-The-Brief-Global-with-AI-Powered-Home-Appliances_Main3.jpg" alt="At "The Brief" events held across Latin America, Samsung introduced AI home technologies that connect various home appliances through SmartThings, allowing users to manage them from one place. Pictured: Events in Brazil (left) and Mexico (right)." class="wp-image-177837" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ At “The Brief” events held across Latin America, Samsung introduced AI home technologies that connect various home appliances through SmartThings, allowing users to manage them from one place. Pictured: Events in Brazil (left) and Mexico (right).</figcaption></figure>



<p>Attendees experienced how SmartThings lets users check the operating status of individual appliances and manage and control them as needed. Samsung emphasized that bringing everyday appliances, such as refrigerators, washing machines, and air conditioners, under one connected system can make life at home significantly more convenient.</p>



<p>Samsung demonstrated the practical benefits of SmartThings-connected appliances designed for local living environments and daily habits.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Southwest Asia: From Personalized Cooling to Family Care, AI Appliances That Look After Everyday Needs</strong></h2>



<p>Personalized cooling and family care were among the highlights of “The Brief,” held September 16 in Gurugram, India, reflecting the region’s hot, humid climate and family-centered lifestyle.</p>



<p>In the air conditioner experience zone, Samsung demonstrated five airflow control modes powered by the “Motion Wind” technology in its Bespoke AI WindFree Pro Air Conditioner. A highlight was the “WindFree<img src="https://s.w.org/images/core/emoji/16.0.1/72x72/2122.png" alt="™" class="wp-smiley" style="height: 1em; max-height: 1em;" /> Wearable Good Sleep” mode, which connects to Galaxy Watch and Galaxy Ring devices to adjust cooling based on users’ sleep stages and changes in body temperature, creating a comfortable sleep environment.</p>



<p>In the kitchen appliance experience zone, Samsung introduced “Now Brief,” which delivers personalized information on the screen of the Bespoke AI Refrigerator Family Hub, and “Family Care,” which monitors family members’ device usage patterns to check on their well-being. Together, these features demonstrated how AI can go beyond simplifying household chores to provide personalized experiences and help look after family members in their daily lives.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="491" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/19162803/Samsung-Corporate-The-Brief-Global-with-AI-Powered-Home-Appliances_Main4.jpg" alt="At "The Brief" held in Gurugram, India, Samsung demonstrated personalized cooling technologies and the "Family Care" feature developed with the local climate and family-centered lifestyle in mind." class="wp-image-177838" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ At “The Brief” held in Gurugram, India, Samsung demonstrated personalized cooling technologies and the “Family Care” feature developed with the local climate and family-centered lifestyle in mind.</figcaption></figure>



<p>Samsung also highlighted its service offerings, including SmartThings Home Care and remote appliance management, which extend support beyond the initial purchase to everyday use, maintenance, and ongoing care.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Southeast Asia: Expanding Appliance Solutions from Homes to Businesses</strong></h2>



<p>The final stop was Hanoi, Vietnam, where Samsung unveiled a broad range of appliance solutions for both individual consumers and business customers at “The Brief Hanoi” on October 14. The event brought together leading media representatives and influencers from six Southeast Asian countries.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="750" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/19163055/Samsung-Corporate-The-Brief-Global-with-AI-Powered-Home-Appliances_Main5.jpg" alt="At "The Brief Hanoi" in Hanoi, Vietnam, Samsung showcased a wide range of products and technologies, from AI-powered home appliances to HVAC solutions, expanding its presence across new market segments." class="wp-image-177839" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ At “The Brief Hanoi” in Hanoi, Vietnam, Samsung showcased a wide range of products and technologies, from AI-powered home appliances to HVAC solutions, expanding its presence across new market segments.</figcaption></figure>



<p>The event took place at Samsung’s newly renovated Business Experience Studio (BES). In spaces recreating real-world settings such as schools, hotels, and commercial facilities, attendees explored firsthand how Samsung’s products and solutions can be applied across a variety of business environments.</p>



<p>Samsung placed particular emphasis on its heating, ventilation, and air-conditioning (HVAC) portfolio, highlighting the company’s strengths in this emerging growth business and reinforcing its market position. The company also previewed its 2027 air conditioner lineup, featuring updated designs and AI-powered capabilities such as “AI Energy Mode.”</p>



<p>For residential appliances, Samsung demonstrated AI-powered convenience features, including “AI Vision” and “Bixby.” The company also introduced its approach to ongoing product care, which combines software updates for up to seven years and its “Home Appliance Remote Management (HRM)” service. The HRM service remotely diagnoses potential issues and provides users with guidance. Together, these services help improve appliance functionality and usability long after purchase.</p>



<p>Looking ahead, Samsung plans to further expand its AI appliance offerings and services to reflect the unique living environments, lifestyles, and consumer needs of each region, strengthening its competitive position in the global AI home market.</p> Samsung Art Store Unveils Curated Collection From Art Basel Paris 2026 https://news.samsung.com/global/samsung-art-store-unveils-curated-collection-from-art-basel-paris-2026 Tue, 20 Oct 2026 08:00:00 +0000 Samsung Newsroom Press Releases TVs & Displays Art Basel Art Basel Paris 2026 Samsung Art Store Samsung Art TV https://bit.ly/3VWE5PV Samsung Electronics, a global display leader and provider of the Official Art TV of Art Basel, today announced the expansion of its Art Basel collection on Samsung Art Store with 19 new artworks from galleries participating in Art Basel Paris 2026. Available starting today on all Samsung Art TVs in every market with Samsung Art […] <figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="606" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/19133217/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Samsung-Art-TV-Art-Basel-Paris-2026-Collection_Main1.jpg" alt="“Poppies #4” by Elene Shatberashvili on Samsung OLED S95H" class="wp-image-177827" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ <em>“Poppies #4” by Elene Shatberashvili on Samsung OLED S95H</em><sup data-fn="7d7701a4-8019-4aaa-8ad9-405184759d75" class="fn"><a href="#7d7701a4-8019-4aaa-8ad9-405184759d75" id="7d7701a4-8019-4aaa-8ad9-405184759d75-link">1</a></sup></figcaption></figure>



<p>Samsung Electronics, a global display leader and provider of the Official Art TV of Art Basel, today announced the expansion of its Art Basel collection on Samsung Art Store with 19 new artworks from galleries participating in Art Basel Paris 2026. Available starting today on all Samsung Art TVs in every market with Samsung Art Store,<sup data-fn="06c36864-6629-43f0-9d13-7a6608566327" class="fn"><a href="#06c36864-6629-43f0-9d13-7a6608566327" id="06c36864-6629-43f0-9d13-7a6608566327-link">2</a></sup> the additions offer audiences more ways to discover and enjoy art at home.</p>



<p>Launching ahead of the fair at the Grand Palais, the selection builds on Samsung’s ongoing collaboration with Art Basel. The new works join the existing Art Basel collection on the platform, extending opportunities for discovery beyond the fair itself.</p>



<p>“What interests me about the Art Basel Paris Collection is how differently artists can transform familiar subjects — through painted color or an entire sculpted world,” said Daria Greene, Global Head of Content & Curation for Samsung Art Store. “Bringing those approaches together on Samsung Art Store gives viewers a chance to discover the range of artistic imagination within their own homes.”</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Clearly Depicted Artwork, Whether Paintings or Sculpted Scenes</strong></h2>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="626" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/19133624/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Samsung-Art-TV-Art-Basel-Paris-2026-Collection_Main2.jpg" alt="“The Lounge (black and white)” by Hans Op de Beeck on Samsung The Frame Pro" class="wp-image-177828" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ <em>“The Lounge (black and white)” by Hans Op de Beeck on Samsung The Frame Pro</em></figcaption></figure>



<p>Highlights include Elene Shatberashvili’s “Poppies #4,” which brings the artist’s expressive use of color and close observation of everyday subjects to the collection. Additionally, Hans Op de Beeck’s “The Lounge (black and white)” introduces a distinctive monochrome world that depicts a meticulously constructed sculptural interior suspended in time.</p>



<p>Presented on Samsung Art Store, the works bring two different artistic processes to the screen: the color and brushwork of painting and the form, texture, and light of a sculpted scene. Viewed on The Frame and other Samsung Art TVs,<sup data-fn="510b62d3-403a-4d26-9332-a650e134b016" class="fn"><a href="#510b62d3-403a-4d26-9332-a650e134b016" id="510b62d3-403a-4d26-9332-a650e134b016-link">3</a></sup> they offer contrasting ways to experience familiar subjects within the home — from the vivid presence of flowers to the imagined depth of an interior.</p>



<p>“Discovery is at the heart of Art Basel, and galleries play an essential role in introducing audiences to artists and their work,” said Karim Crippa, Director of Art Basel Paris. “Our collaboration with Samsung Art Store extends the reach of those encounters, giving audiences around the world a way to explore a selection from participating galleries and continue engaging with art beyond the Paris fair.”</p>



<p>For visitors to Art Basel Paris, the expanded collection offers a way to continue exploring select works at home. For audiences elsewhere, it provides an opportunity to discover art from participating galleries without travelling to the fair. On Samsung Art Store, users can explore the additions alongside the existing Art Basel collection, return to their favorites, and choose works to display in their living spaces.</p>



<p>The Art Basel Paris 2026 additions are available on Samsung Art Store starting today on all Samsung Art TVs in markets in which the platform is available. To learn more, visit <a href="https://news.samsung.com/global/samsung-art-store" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">https://news.samsung.com/global/samsung-art-store</a></p>



<hr class="wp-block-separator has-alpha-channel-opacity" />



<h4 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>About Art Basel</strong></h4>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading">Founded in 1970 by gallerists from Basel, Art Basel today stages the world’s premier art shows for Modern and contemporary art, sited in Basel, Miami Beach, Hong Kong, Paris, and Qatar. Defined by its host city and region, each show is unique, which is reflected in its participating galleries, artworks presented, and the content of parallel programming produced in collaboration with local institutions for each edition. Art Basel’s engagement has expanded beyond art fairs through new digital platforms including the Art Basel App and initiatives such as the Art Basel and UBS Global Art Market Report, the Art Basel Shop, and the Art Basel Awards. For further information, please visit <a href="https://www.artbasel.com/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">artbasel.com.</a><a></a></h4>

The campaign has been featured through out-of-home (OOH) advertising at prominent locations, including Times Square in New York, Piccadilly Circus in London, […] <p>Samsung Electronics is expanding its SSD brand campaign, <strong>“Choose the sure thing,”</strong> across major global landmarks in New York, London, and Hong Kong, creating more opportunities to connect with consumers around the world.</p>



<p>The campaign has been featured through out-of-home (OOH) advertising at prominent locations, including <strong>Times Square in New York, Piccadilly Circus in London, and the Entertainment Building in Central, Hong Kong.</strong></p>

<p>The exhibition features 14 artworks inspired by Samsung products, displayed on the 2026 OLED TVs (model: S95H) fitted with frames customized for each work.</p>

<p>“Through the eyes of different creative minds, familiar products become new expressions of personality, perspective, and ways of life,” said Mauro Porcini, President and Chief Design Officer of the Device eXperience (DX) Division at Samsung Electronics. “By bringing these interpretations together, we want to show how design can make technology a more personal part of everyday life. Ultimately, our ambition is to move beyond designing technology for people to designing technology that truly reflects them.”</p>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>14 Italian Artists Reinterpret Samsung Products Through Creativity and Imagination</strong></h2>

<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>OLED TVs Showcase Expressive Design With Frames Customized for Each Artwork</strong></h2>



<p>The exhibition features 14 Samsung OLED TVs (model S95H), each fitted with a custom frame designed to complement its corresponding artwork’s colors and mood. Created specifically for the exhibition, the frames work together with the on-screen imagery to enhance the presentation of each work.</p>



<p>The Float Layer Design of the OLED TV (model: S95H) makes the screen appear to float above a metal plate. Together, the TV’s distinctive design and custom frames create a visual presentation that highlights the character of each artwork.</p>



<p>Visitors can experience Expressive Design — an approach that embraces human diversity and imagination — through the varied combinations of artworks and frames on the same TV model.</p>



<p>The exhibition will take place from Oct. 15-25 at Dongdaemun Design Plaza (DDP) in Seoul.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/15093259/Samsung-Corporate-Seoul-Design-Week-2026-Design-is-an-Act-of-Love_Main3.jpg" alt="An image of Seoul Design Week 2026" class="wp-image-177791" /></figure> From Smart City to Race Circuit: Samsung’s The Wall in Control Rooms https://news.samsung.com/global/from-smart-city-to-race-circuit-samsungs-the-wall-in-control-rooms Thu, 15 Oct 2026 08:00:00 +0000 Samsung Newsroom TVs & Displays Control Room Smart City The Wall https://bit.ly/4s0cFEF On the front lines of crisis management, where timely decisions are critical, control rooms are evolving to meet the demands of today’s highly connected society. Moving beyond traditional monitoring, they have become centralized hubs that manage growing volumes of video and data while overseeing communications networks. From industrial operations to sports and critical infrastructure, organizations […] <p>On the front lines of crisis management, where timely decisions are critical, control rooms are evolving to meet the demands of today’s highly connected society. Moving beyond traditional monitoring, they have become centralized hubs that manage growing volumes of video and data while overseeing communications networks.</p>



<p>From industrial operations to sports and critical infrastructure, organizations around the world are turning to Samsung’s The Wall. Among them are the Iskandar Puteri Command and Control Centre (IPCCC), the integrated operations center for Iskandar Puteri City Council, Johor, Malaysia; and NASCAR, one of the world’s leading motorsports organizations.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/14142029/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Samsung-The-Wall-Control-Rooms_main1.jpg" alt="The Wall at the Iskandar Puteri Command and Control Centre’s control room" class="wp-image-177742" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The Wall at the Iskandar Puteri Command and Control Centre’s control room</figcaption></figure>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong>Engineered for Mission-Critical Control Rooms</strong></h2>



<p>While the information displayed on screen varies across industries, every control room requires robust security and unwavering reliability.</p>



<p>The Wall uses the industry-standard Advanced Encryption Standard 128-bit (AES-128) encryption algorithm and Samsung’s proprietary Knox Security platform to securely encrypt data transmitted from S-Box to Cabinet.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="563" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/14142124/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Samsung-The-Wall-Control-Rooms_main2.jpg" alt="The Wall uses the industry-standard Advanced Encryption Standard 128-bit (AES-128) encryption algorithm and Samsung’s proprietary Knox Security platform to securely encrypt data transmitted from S-Box to Cabinet." class="wp-image-177743" /></figure>



<p>AES-128, a symmetric-key encryption algorithm adopted by the U.S. National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST), protects sensitive control room information while enabling fast data transmission and reception.</p>



<p>Designed for uninterrupted 24/7 operation, The Wall incorporates redundant power and signal systems. If an unexpected power failure or signal error occurs, the backup system activates immediately to ensure continuous monitoring and control.</p>



<p>The Wall’s modular chip-on-board (COB) architecture allows individual modules to be replaced quickly while the display remains on, minimizing downtime. With an LED lifespan<sup data-fn="f745fd76-eceb-4483-9bb0-7710d6e1237c" class="fn"><a href="#f745fd76-eceb-4483-9bb0-7710d6e1237c" id="f745fd76-eceb-4483-9bb0-7710d6e1237c-link">1</a></sup> of up to 150,000 hours — among the longest in the industry – it also helps reduce long-term total cost of ownership (TCO).</p>



<p>Optimized for control room environments, The Wall offers a modular, scalable design that can be customized to virtually any space. Advanced display technology ensures large volumes of information are presented with clarity and precision.</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><a href="https://bit.ly/3PP2yUA" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><strong>IPCCC</strong></a><strong>: Cross-Agency Coordination for Faster Emergency Response</strong></h2>



<p>Urban crises often unfold as a chain of interconnected events. For example, if a water main bursts, traffic may need to be redirected immediately. If a major traffic accident occurs, firefighters conducting rescue operations and police managing the scene must coordinate in real time.</p>



<p>In Johor, Malaysia, IPCCC uses Samsung’s The Wall to support integrated city monitoring and coordination among relevant agencies. IPCCC selected The Wall for its image quality, color richness, and brightness, as well as Samsung’s reputation as a reliable long-term technology partner.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-embed is-type-video is-provider-youtube wp-block-embed-youtube wp-embed-aspect-16-9 wp-has-aspect-ratio"><div class="wp-block-embed__wrapper">
<iframe loading="lazy" title="Display Solutions: Case Study - The Wall x MBIP | Samsung" width="500" height="281" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/Gvl43-qiVBg?feature=oembed" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin" allowfullscreen></iframe>
</div></figure>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading has-text-align-center">▲ The Wall supports IPCCC’s city operations monitoring with an immersive visual experience.</h4>



<p>IPCCC brings together traffic, public safety, and environmental monitoring data onto a single, unified display. Anchoring the operations center is Malaysia’s longest The Wall installation, spanning 19.35 meters and capturing the city’s vast network of information on one screen.</p>



<p>Today, the massive display serves as the command hub for citywide operations — from security monitoring to coordinated incident response. By consolidating this information, The Wall supports stronger inter-agency coordination across departments.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="562" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/14142411/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Samsung-The-Wall-Control-Rooms_main3.jpg" alt="The Wall helps IPCCC city operations teams monitor traffic, public safety, and city services." class="wp-image-177745" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The Wall helps IPCCC city operations teams monitor traffic, public safety, and city services.</figcaption></figure>



<p>“Installing a curved large-scale display allows us to maximize the space while maintaining an efficient operational layout for our workstation and war room,” said Farah Izzati Binti Muhamad Fuad, Director of the Smart City Unit at IPCCC. “The ultra-wide screen enables us to monitor multiple smart safety systems, improving coordination and allowing our team to respond more quickly to incidents.”</p>



<p></p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading"><strong><a href="https://news.samsung.com/us/samsung-fuels-nascar-productions-remote-race-control-room/" data-type="link" data-id="https://news.samsung.com/us/samsung-fuels-nascar-productions-remote-race-control-room/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">NASCAR</a>: Race Control for Split-Second Decisions</strong></h2>



<p>Beyond public services, control systems also play a critical role in sports — where capturing every moment of the action is essential. In motorsports, where speeds exceed 290 kilometers per hour and up to 40 cars race in close formation, officials must assess incidents and make decisions in fractions of a second.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-image size-full"><img loading="lazy" width="1000" height="667" src="https://img.global.news.samsung.com/global/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/14142549/Samsung-TVs-and-Displays-Samsung-The-Wall-Control-Rooms_main4.jpg" alt="The Wall provides NASCAR’s Remote Race Control center with a large, clear view of live race data, video feeds, and on-track conditions." class="wp-image-177746" /><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">▲ The Wall provides NASCAR’s Remote Race Control center with a large, clear view of live race data, video feeds, and on-track conditions.</figcaption></figure>



<p>NASCAR, the most popular motorsport in the U.S., built its Remote Race Control center around The Wall to support race control staff at the track with additional monitoring, analysis, and replay capabilities. Measuring 9.75 meters wide and 2.74 meters high, the massive display provides up to 24 officials a shared, high-resolution view, giving them the visual clarity to review and manage split-second race incidents together.</p>



<p>Equipped with a Micro AI Processor, The Wall analyzes complex live footage in real time to reduce noise. This enables dynamic camera feeds from around the track to be displayed in ultra-high definition with minimal noise and motion blur, helping officials make fast, accurate decisions.</p>



<p>At the launch in 2025, Steve Stum, NASCAR Vice President of Operations and Technical Production, highlighted the visibility that Remote Race Control would provide through more than 200 camera angles and multiple data points from every car, along with its potential to enable faster, more accurate competition calls and improve the racing product. Now in its second season of operation, the remote race control room gives officials greater visibility across races and supports more efficient and accurate officiating.</p>



<p>By consolidating multiple video feeds and data streams on The Wall, the remote race control room enables officials to view more information simultaneously and share feeds for collaborative review.</p>



<p>Describing the experience, Scott Miller, competition strategist at NASCAR, said, “It’s like having 20 monitors in front of you, as opposed to probably two or three sitting at a single work station.”</p>



<figure class="wp-block-embed is-type-video is-provider-youtube wp-block-embed-youtube wp-embed-aspect-16-9 wp-has-aspect-ratio"><div class="wp-block-embed__wrapper">
<iframe loading="lazy" title="NASCAR and Samsung reimagine race control" width="500" height="281" src="https://www.youtube.com/embed/MTKyS5NGWAw?feature=oembed" frameborder="0" allow="accelerometer; autoplay; clipboard-write; encrypted-media; gyroscope; picture-in-picture; web-share" referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin" allowfullscreen></iframe>
</div></figure>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading has-text-align-center">▲ Samsung technology supports NASCAR’s Remote Race Control center at its production facility in Concord, North Carolina.</h4>



<p>Control rooms across industries are evolving to meet increasingly complex demands — whether ensuring the stable operation of infrastructure, enabling integrated citywide monitoring, or supporting live sports where decisions made in fractions of a second can make all the difference. Regardless of the application, every control room depends on a display that helps operators process vast amounts of information and make fast, accurate decisions. As a safe and reliable visual hub for mission-critical operations, Samsung’s The Wall will continue to provide the clarity needed to support decision-making across major industries worldwide.</p>
